 \section{Auxiliary results}\label{auxiliary}

In this section we collect some technical results. Their proofs are gathered in section~\ref{sec:proofauxi}. 

\begin{lem}\label{lemma:3tailbounds}\textbf{Tail bounds on the maximum and minimum of folded Gaussians} 
\begin{enumerate}[a)]
    \item if $\bs y\sim N_p\left(0,\frac{1}{n}I_n \right)$, $p>1$, and $a \geq \sqrt{\frac{2\ln(p)}{n}}$, $$P\left(\underset{i\in\{1,\ldots,p\}}{\max}|y_i|> a \right) \leq \frac{e^{-\frac{n}{2}\left(a^2-\frac{2\ln (p)}{n}\right)^{2}}}{\sqrt{\pi\ln (p)}}$$
    \item if $\bs y\sim N_s\left(\bs\beta,\sigma^2\right)$, $s>1$, and $a \leq \underset{i\in\{1,\ldots,s\}}{\min}|\beta_i|$,
        $$P\left(\underset{i\in\{1,\ldots,s\}}{\min}|y_i| > a\right) \geq P\left(\underset{i\in\{1,\ldots,s\}}{\min}|\beta_i|-\underset{i\in\{1,\ldots,s\}}{\max}|y_i-\beta_i| > a \right)$$
    \item if $\forall i\in\{1,\ldots,s\}$ $y_i\sim N\left(\mu,\frac{1}{n}\right)$, $a<|\mu|$
        $$P\left(\underset{i\in\{1,\ldots,s\}}{\min}|y_i| > a \right) \leq \exp\left\{-\frac{s}{2}\frac{e^{-2n(|\mu|-a)^2/\pi}-e^{-n(a+|\mu|)^2/2}}{\left(1-e^{-2n(|\mu|-a)^2/\pi}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\left(1-e^{-n(a+|\mu|)^2/2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}}\right\} $$
\end{enumerate}
\end{lem}


% \section{Proof of Theorem~\ref{theo:suffcondlinearmodel}}

\begin{lem}\label{lem:L0toL1convergencegen} For $\hat S^b$ as in \eqref{eq:Mhat} and any $k$ models $M_1,\ldots,M_k$
$$P(\hat{S}^b\not\in \{M_1,\ldots,M_k\})\;\leq\; (k+1) \sum_{M \in \mathcal{M}\setminus \{M_1,\ldots,M_k\}}\E\left( NC(M)\right).$$
\end{lem}

\begin{lem}\label{lem:boundrhogen}
 For any $M\in\mathcal{M}$ and $T\subseteq S$, let $Q_T=M \cup T$. The non-centrality parameter defined in \eqref{eq:muQM} satisfies: 
\begin{equation}
    \mu_{Q_TM}\; \geq \;n\,\rho(\bs X)\,\sum_{j=1}^b|T_j\setminus M_j|\,\min_{i\in T_j\setminus M_j}{\beta_{i}^{\star}}^2
\end{equation}
\end{lem}

\begin{lem}\label{lemma:s1s3}
Let $W \sim \chi_\nu^2(\mu)$. If $\mu=0$ and $w>\nu$, then 
$$P(W>w) \leq\left(\frac{e w}{\nu}\right)^{\frac{\nu}{2}} e^{-w / 2}.$$
If $\mu>0$ and $w>\mu+\nu$, then
$$
P(W>w) \leq e^{-\frac{w}{2}\left(1-\sqrt{\frac{\mu}{w}}\right)^2}\left(\frac{w}{\mu}\right)^{\frac{\nu}{4}} .
$$
\end{lem}

\begin{lem}\label{lemma:s2} Let $W \sim \chi_\nu^2(\mu)$ with $\mu>0$. If $w<\mu$, then $
P(W<w) \leq \frac{\exp \left\{-\frac{1}{2}(\sqrt{\mu}-\sqrt{w})^2\right\}}{(\mu / w)^{\nu / 4}}.
$
\end{lem}


\begin{lem}\label{lemma:s18} 
    Let  $W\sim \chi_\nu^2(\mu)$, $g>0$, $\bar{u}>0$ and $\underline{u}\in(0,1)$. If $\mu=0$, and $\left(1+ge^{-\nu/2}\right)^{-1}\leq\underline{u}<\bar{u}$, we have
$$
\int_{\underline{u}}^{\bar{u}} P\left(W>2 \ln \left(\frac{g}{1 / u-1}\right)\right) d u\;\;<\;\;\left(\frac{e}{\nu}\right)^{\nu\over2}\left[2 \ln \left(\frac{g}{1 / \bar{u}-1}\right)\right]^{\nu\over2} \frac{1}{g}\ln \left(\frac{1}{\underline{u}}\right) .
$$
    If $\mu>0$, $1>\bar{u}>\underline{u}\geq\left(1+g^{\gamma}e^{-\nu/2}\right)^{-1}$ for some $\gamma\in(0,1)$ and $(1-\gamma)\ln(g)>\mu$, we have
$$
\int_{\underline{u}}^{\bar{u}} P\left(W>2 \ln \left(\frac{g}{1 / u-1}\right)\right) d u<\left[2 \ln \left(\frac{g}{1 / \bar{u}-1}\right)\right]^{\frac{\nu}{4}} \frac{\mu^{-\nu/4}\Big[\bar{u}-\underline{u}+2l(\bar{u})\ln\Big(\frac{\bar{u}}{\underline{u}}\Big)\Big]}{g^{2l(\underline{u})}} 
$$
where for any $u\geq\left(1+g^{\gamma}e^{-\nu/2}\right)^{-1}$, $l(u)=\frac{1}{2}\Big(1-\sqrt{\mu\big[2\ln\big(g(u^{-1}-1)^{-1}\big)\big]^{-1}}\Big)^2$
\end{lem}

\begin{lem}\label{lemma:s20}
Let $g>0$ and $W \sim \chi_\nu^2(\mu)$ where $\nu$ may depend on $g$. If $\mu=0$ and $\ln(g) \geq 3\nu/4$, then $$
\int_0^1 P\left(W>2 \ln \left(\frac{g}{1 / u-1}\right)\right) d u\;\; \leq\;\; \frac{16}{g}\left[\ln \left(g\right)\right]^{\frac{\nu}{2}+1} $$
If $\mu\geq 1$ and $\phi\ln(g) \geq \max\{7,\nu/2\}$ for some $\phi < \Big(1-\big(\frac{\mu}{2\ln(g)}\big)^{\frac{1}{3}}\Big)^2<1$ then
$$
\int_0^1 P\left(W>2 \ln \left(\frac{g}{1 / u-1}\right)\right) d u\;\; \leq\;\; \frac{2}{g^{\phi}} 
\left[2\ln\big(g^{(\phi+1)}\big)\right]^{\frac{\nu}{4}+1} $$
\end{lem}





\begin{lem}\label{lem:boundexpnc} For $M\in \mathcal{M}\setminus \{S\}$ denote $A_S:=\gamma\Delta_{MS}+\tfrac{1-\gamma}{8}\mu_{Q_SM}$ (cf \eqref{eq:DeltaM} and \eqref{eq:muQM}). Suppose that $A_S\geq \frac{3}{4}|M\setminus S|$ for some $\gamma \in (1/2,1]$. Then
$$\E\left(NC(M)\right)  \;\;\leq\;\;  16
e^{-A_S}A_S^{\frac{|M\setminus S|}{2}+1} + e^{-A}.$$
\end{lem}

\begin{lem}\label{lem:boundexpnc2} Let $T\in\mathcal{T}$ and $M\in \mathcal{M}$ such that $M\neq T$ and $M\cap S^I=T\cap S^I$. Denote $Q_T= M\cup T$ and
$A_T:=\gamma\Delta_{MT}+\tfrac{1-\gamma}{8}\mu_{Q_TM}$ (cf \eqref{eq:DeltaM} and \eqref{eq:muQM}). Suppose that $\phi A_T\geq \max\{7,\frac{3}{4}|M\setminus T|\}$ for some $\gamma \in (1/2,1]$ and $\phi \in (0,1)$. Then if $M \cap S^S = \emptyset$, we have:
$$\E\left(NC(M)\right)  \;\;\leq\;\;  16
e^{-A_T}A_T^{\frac{|M\setminus S|}{2}+1} + e^{-A_T}.$$
and if $M \cap S^S\neq\emptyset$, $\phi < \Big(1-\big(\frac{\mu_{Q_TT}}{2A_T}\big)^{\frac{1}{3}}\Big)^2$ and $\mu_{Q_TT}>1$, we have:
$$\E\left(NC(M)\right)  \;\;\leq\;\;  2e^{-\phi A_T}[2(\phi+1)\,A_T]^{\frac{|M\setminus T|}{4}+1}+ e^{-A_T}$$

\end{lem}

\begin{lem}\label{lem:upperboundnoncentralgen}
    For any $T\subseteq S$ and $M\in \mathcal{M}$, let $Q_T=M\cup T$, $$\mu_{Q_TT} \leq n\,\bar{\lambda}\,\sum_{j=1}^b|(S_j\cap M_j )\setminus T_j|\,\max_{i\in (S_j\cap M_j )\setminus T_j}{\beta_{i}^{\star}}^2 \quad \text{where}\quad \bar{\lambda}\,:=\,\lambda_{\max}\Big(\frac1n \bs X_{S}^\top\bs X_{S}\Big)$$
    and
    $$\mu_{Q_TT} \geq n\,\lambda_{\min}\Big(\frac1n \bs X_{S}^\top\bs X_{S}\Big)\,\sum_{j=1}^b|(S_j\cap M_j )\setminus T_j|\,\min_{i\in (S_j\cap M_j )\setminus T_j}{\beta_{i}^{\star}}^2$$
\end{lem}

\begin{lem}\label{lem:convtoTprob}
    Assume A1, A2 and A6 hold and $|S^I|=O(1)$ (cf \eqref{eq:defslsi}), then $P(\hat{S}^b \in \mathcal{T})\to 1$.
\end{lem}


\section{Proofs of Section~\ref{sec:properties_orth}}


\subsection{Proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:useful-reformulation-GSM}}
First note that by discarding constant term 
 $$\arg\max_{M\in \mathcal{M}} \left\{\max_{\beta\in \mathcal{L}_M}\ell(\bs y; \bs \beta)-\sum_{j=1}^b\kappa_j |M_j| \right\}=\arg\min_{M\in \mathcal{M}} \left\{\min_{\beta\in \mathcal{L}_M}\bigg\{ \tfrac{1}{2}\|\bs y-\sqrt{n}\bs\beta\|^2\right\}+\sum_{j=1}^b\kappa_j |M_j| \bigg\}$$
 
 We also have $\min_{\beta\in \mathcal{L}_M} \tfrac{1}{2}\|\bs y-\sqrt{n}\bs\beta\|^2=\tfrac{1}{2}\|\bs y-\sqrt{n}\tilde{\bs \beta}_M\|^2=\tfrac{1}{2}\|\bs y\|^2-\tfrac{1}{2}\|\sqrt{n}\tilde{\bs \beta}_M\|^2$. The minimization in \eqref{eq:Mhat} can be then replaced with maximization of $\tfrac{1}{2}
 \|\sqrt{n}\tilde{\bs \beta}_M\|^2-\sum_{j=1}^b \kappa_j|M_j|$.

\subsection{Proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:isthresh}}
	Under the assumptions, we can write 
	$$
	\tfrac{1}{2}\|\sqrt{n}\tilde{\bs \beta}_M\|^2-\sum_{j=1}^b\kappa_j |M_j|\;=\;\sum_{j=1}^b\sum_{i\in M_j}(\tfrac{n}{2}{\tilde{ \beta}_i}^2-\kappa_j).
	$$
	Now, Lemma~\ref{lem:useful-reformulation-GSM} gives that \eqref{eq:MhatGSM} can be maximized with respect to each $M_j$ by including $i\in \hat S_j$ whenever $n\tilde\beta_i^2\geq 2\kappa_j$.

\subsection{Proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:l1isthreshold}}
	The MLE under the full model is $\tilde{\bs\beta}=\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{n}} \bs y$ and the optimized function in \eqref{eq:genl1} can be then rewritten as
	$$
	\tfrac{1}{2}\bs y^{\top}\bs y +\tfrac{n}{2}\sum_{j=1}^b\sum_{i\in B_j}\left(\beta_i^2-2\tilde\beta_i\beta_i+2\tfrac{\lambda_j}{n}|\beta_i|\right)
	$$
	which we optimize with respect to each $\beta_i$ separately. For any $i\in B_j$ we have $\hat\beta_i=0$ if and only if $|\tilde \beta_i|\leq \frac{\lambda_j}{n}$. Similarly  for \eqref{eq:genl1ada}, for any $i\in B_j$ we have $\hat\beta_i=0$ if and only if $|\tilde \beta_i|\leq \frac{\lambda_j}{|\beta^{\circ}_i|n}$. If $\bs \beta^{\circ}= \tilde{\bs\beta}$, then for any $i\in B_j$ $\hat{\beta}_i = 0$ if and only if $|\tilde \beta_i|\leq \sqrt{\tfrac{\lambda_j}{n}}$. If $\bs\beta^\circ$ is a LASSO estimate with penalization $\lambda^\circ$, then for any $i\in B_j$ 
 $\hat{\beta}_i = 0$ if and only if $\tilde{\beta}^2_i + \operatorname{sign}(\lambda^{\circ}/n-\tilde{\beta}_i) \frac{\lambda^\circ}{n}\tilde{\beta}_i-\frac{\lambda_j}{n} \leq 0$. Equivalently, for any $i\in B_j$ 
 $\hat{\beta}_i = 0$ if and only if $|\tilde{\beta}_ i| \leq  \frac{1}{2}\left(\lambda^\circ/n+\sqrt{(\lambda^\circ/n)^2+4 \lambda_j/n}\right)$.


\subsection{Proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:suffnecIblockthresh}} By the union bound and by Lemma~\ref{lemma:3tailbounds} a), for $n\geq n_0$, \begin{equation*}
P\left(\hat{S}^{T,b} \not\subseteq S\right)\;\leq\; \sum_{j=1}^b P\Big(\max_{i\in B_j\setminus S_j}|y_i/\sqrt{n}|>\tau_{j}\Big)\;\leq\;\sum_{j=1}^b\frac{e^{-\tfrac{n}{2}\left(\tau_j^2-\tfrac{2\ln(p_j-s_j)}{n}\right)^2}}{\sqrt{\pi\ln(p_j-s_j)}} 	.
 \end{equation*}
 By A4, the nominator on the right is bounded above by $1$ for all sufficiently large $n$. It follows that 
	$$P\left(\hat{S}^{T,b} \not\subseteq S\right) \;\leq\; \sum_{j=1}^b (\pi\ln (p_j-s_j))^{-1/2} \to 0\qquad \mbox{as } n\to \infty.$$
For the second statement, by independence, we have 
$$P(\hat{S}^{T,b} \subseteq S)\;=\;\prod_{j=1}^b P(\max_{i \in B_j\setminus S_j}|y_i/\sqrt{n}| \leq \tau_j).$$ 
Consider $j$ that satisfies \eqref{cond:necIallblockthresh0}. In particular, there exists $c<1$ such that, for sufficiently large $n$, we have that $\tau_j\leq c\sqrt{2\ln(p_j-s_j)/n}$. Then, for any such $n$,
$$
P\Big(\max_{i \in B_j\setminus S_j}|y_i/\sqrt{n}| \leq \tau_j\Big)\;\leq\; P\Big(\max_{i \in B_j\setminus S_j}|y_i| \leq c \sqrt{2\ln(p_j-s_j)}\Big)\;\leq\;P\Big(\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{2\ln(p_j-s_j)}}\max_{i \in B_j\setminus S_j}y_i \leq c \Big).
$$
On the other hand, results from extreme value theory, Chapter 4 in \cite{galambos}, show that 
 $$\frac{1}{\sqrt{2 \ln(p_j-s_j)}}\max_{i\in B_j\setminus S_j}y_i \;\overset{P}{\longrightarrow} 1$$
 and so 
 $$
 P\Big(\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\ln(p_j-s_j)}}\max_{i \in B_j\setminus S_j}y_i \leq c \Big)\;\to\;0,
 $$ 
 which implies that $P(\hat{S}^{T,b} \subseteq S)\to 0$. 

\subsection{Proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:suffnecIIblockthresh}}
	 By the union bound, 
	$P\left(S \not\subseteq \hat{S}^{T,b}\right)\leq \sum_{j=1}^b  P\left(\min_{i\in S_j}|y_i/\sqrt{n}|\leq\tau_j\right)$. 
	By Lemma~\ref{lemma:3tailbounds} b), for each $j$,	
	$$P\left(\min_{i\in S_j}|y_i/\sqrt{n}|\leq\tau_j\right)\;\leq\; P\left(\max_{i\in S_j}|\tfrac{y_i}{\sqrt{n}}-\beta_i| \geq\beta_{\min,j}^{\star}-\tau_j \right).$$ By Lemma~\ref{lemma:3tailbounds} a)  
	\begin{equation}\label{eq:rate2}
P\left(\max_{i\in S_j}|\tfrac{y_i}{\sqrt{n}}-\beta_i| \geq\beta_{\min,j}^{\star}-\tau_j \right) \;\leq\;\frac{e^{-\tfrac{n}{2}\left((\beta^\star_{\min,j}-\tau_j)^2-\tfrac{2\ln(s_j)}{n}\right)}}{\sqrt{\pi\ln(s_j)}}\;\leq\; (\pi\ln (s_j))^{-1/2}\;\to\;0,		
	\end{equation}
	which concludes the proof. 

\subsection{Proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:necIIblockthresh}} 
a) By independence we have 
$P(S \subseteq \hat{S}^{T,b})=\prod_{j=1}^b P(\min_{i \in S_j}|y_i/\sqrt{n}| > \tau_j)$. Take any $j$, satisfying \eqref{cond:necIIallblockthresh2}. $P(\min_{i \in S_j}|y_i/\sqrt{n}| > \tau_j)=\prod_{i \in B_j} P(|y_i/\sqrt{n}| > \tau_j)$. Denote $i_{\circ}\in B_j$ the entry, such that $\beta_i^*=\beta^*_{\min,\,j}$. From the proof of Lemma \ref{lemma:3tailbounds} b) $$P(|y_{i_{\circ}}/\sqrt{n}|>\tau_j) 
    = P\left(z>\sqrt{n}\left(\tau_j-\beta_{\min,\,j}^{\star}\right)\right) + P\left(z<-\sqrt{n}\left(\tau_j+\beta_{\min,\,j}^{\star}\right)\right)$$ where $z\sim N(0,1)$. Since $\tau_j<\beta^\star_{\min,j}$ satisfies condition \eqref{cond:necIallblockthresh} $P(z<-\sqrt{n}\left(\tau_j+\beta_{\min,\,j}^{\star}\right)) \to 0$. By \eqref{cond:necIIallblockthresh2} $\underset{n \to +\infty}{\lim}P\left(z>\sqrt{n}\left(\tau_j-\beta_{\min,\,j}^{\star}\right)\right)<1$ and $\underset{n \to +\infty}{\lim}P\left(S_j \subseteq \hat{S}^{T,b} \right)<1$.\\
b)	Take any $j=1,\ldots,b$ satisfying \eqref{cond:necIIallblockthresh} By Lemma~\ref{lemma:3tailbounds} c), since $\beta^\star_{\min,j}>\tau_j$:
$$P\left(\min_{i \in S_j}|\frac{y_i}{\sqrt{n}}| > \tau_j \right)\;\leq\;\exp\left\{-\frac{s_j}{2}\frac{e^{-\tfrac{2n}{\pi}(\beta^\star_{\min,j}-\tau_j)^2}-e^{-\tfrac{n}{2}(\tau_j+\beta^\star_{\min,j})^2}}{\left(1-e^{-2n(\beta^\star_{\min,j}-\tau_j)^2/\pi}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\left(1-e^{-n(\tau_j+\beta^\star_{\min,j})^2/2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}}\right\}. $$
Let $a_{n}=2\left(1-e^{-2n(\beta^\star_{\min,j}-\tau_j)^2/\pi}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+2\left(1-e^{-n(\tau_j+\beta^\star_{\min,j})^2/2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}$ and note that $a_n\in (0, 4]$.  Thus, to show that $P\left(\min_{i \in S_j}|\tfrac{y_i}{\sqrt{n}}| > \tau_j \right)$ is bounded away from $1$, it is enough to show that 
$$
\lim_{n\to \infty}{s_j}({e^{-2n(\beta^\star_{\min,j}-\tau_j)^2/\pi}-e^{-n(\tau_j+\beta^\star_{\min,j})^2/2}})\;>\;0.
$$
By \eqref{cond:necIIallblockthresh}, $\lim {s_j} e^{-2n(\beta^\star_{\min,j}-\tau_j)^2/\pi}\geq 1$. To conclude, we show that there exists $c\in(0,1)$ such that
$$
\lim {s_j} e^{-n(\tau_j+\beta^\star_{\min,j})^2/2}\;\leq\;c\;<\;1,
$$ 
where $c$ is as defined in the proposition. We equivalently need
$\sqrt{n}(\tau_j+\beta^\star_{\min,j})\geq\sqrt{{2\ln (s_j/c)}}$ for all $n$ sufficiently large. To show that, note that $\tau_j\geq \beta^\star_{\min,j}$ and $s_j/c\leq p_j$, which gives
$$
\frac{\tau_j+\beta^\star_{\min,j}}{\sqrt{\tfrac{2\ln(s_j/c)}{n}}}\;\geq\;\frac{2\tau_j}{\sqrt{\tfrac{2\ln(p_j)}{n}}}\;=\;\frac{\sqrt{\tfrac{2\ln(p_j-s_j)}{n}}}{\sqrt{\tfrac{2\ln(p_j)}{n}}}\frac{2\tau_j}{\sqrt{\tfrac{2\ln(p_j-s_j)}{n}}}\;=\;\sqrt{\frac{\ln(p_j-s_j)}{\ln(p_j)}}\frac{2\tau_j}{\sqrt{\tfrac{2\ln(p_j-s_j)}{n}}}.
$$
Since the second term on the right converges to something $\geq 2$, it is enough to show that the first term converges to something $>1/2$. Using our assumption that $\tfrac{s_j}{p_j}<1$, we can take $c$ such that $\tfrac{s_j}{p_j}\leq c<1$ and we get
$$
\sqrt{\frac{\ln(p_j-s_j)}{\ln(p_j)}}\;\geq\;\sqrt{\frac{\ln(p_j)+\ln(1-c)}{\ln(p_j)}}\;\to\;1,
$$
which concludes the proof.

\subsection{Proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:necbetaminblockthresh}}
    We have $\frac{\beta_{\min,j}^*}{\sqrt{\tfrac{2\ln(p_j-s_j)}{n}}+\sqrt{\tfrac{\pi}{2}\tfrac{\ln(s_j)}{n}}}= \frac{\beta_{\min,j}^*-\tau_j+\tau_j}{\sqrt{\tfrac{2\ln(p_j-s_j)}{n}}+\sqrt{\tfrac{\pi}{2}\tfrac{\ln(s_j)}{n}}}$ and \eqref{eq:necbetaminblockthresh} implies that $\lim_{n\to \infty}\frac{\tau_j}{\sqrt{\tfrac{2\ln(p_j-s_j)}{n}}} <1$ or $\lim_{n\to \infty}\frac{\beta_{\min,j}^*-\tau_j}{\sqrt{\tfrac{\pi}{2}\tfrac{\ln(s_j)}{n}}} <1$. 
    If $\lim_{n\to \infty}\frac{\tau_j}{\sqrt{\tfrac{2\ln(p_j-s_j)}{n}}} <1$ by Proposition~\ref{prop:suffnecIblockthresh}, $P(\hat{S}^{T,b} = S)\not \to 1$. If $\beta_{\min,j}<\tau_j$ recovery is not possible either by definition. 
    The last case to examine is when $\beta_{\min,j}\geq\tau_j$, $\lim_{n\to \infty}\frac{\tau_j}{\sqrt{\tfrac{2\ln(p_j-s_j)}{n}}} \geq 1$ and $\lim_{n\to \infty}\frac{\beta_{\min,j}^*-\tau_j}{\sqrt{\tfrac{\pi}{2}\tfrac{\ln(s_j)}{n}}} <1$. By Proposition~\ref{prop:necIIblockthresh}, in that case $P(S \subseteq \hat{S}^{T,b} )\not \to 1$ and recovery is not possible asymptotically.

\subsection{Proof of Theorem~\ref{theo:fullrecovGS}}
We start by showing that if \eqref{eq:betamin} holds then $\tau^\star_j$ satisfies A4 and A5. Under \eqref{eq:betamin}, there exists $c>1$ such that $\beta^\star_{\min,j}=c(\sqrt{\tfrac{2\ln(p_j-s_j)}{n}} + \sqrt{\tfrac{2\ln(s_j)}{n}})$. Denote $a=\sqrt{\tfrac{2\ln(p_j-s_j)}{n}}$ and $b=\sqrt{\tfrac{2\ln(s_j)}{n}}$. We have
$$
\tau_j^\star\;=\;\frac{c}{2} (a+b)+\frac{(a^2-b^2)}{2c(a+b)}\;=\;\frac{c}{2} (a+b)+\frac{a-b}{2c}\;=\;\frac{c^2+1}{2c}a+\frac{c^2-1}{2c}b.
$$
The fact that $\tau^\star_j>a$ (the lower bound in A4) is now clear because $\tfrac{c^2+1}{2c}> 1$  and $\tfrac{c^2-1}{2c}> 0$. To show that the upper bound in A5 observe that
$$
\beta_{\min,j}^* - \tau^*_j \;=\; c(a+b)- \frac{c^2+1}{2c}a+\frac{c^2-1}{2c}b \;= \; \frac{c^2+1}{2c}a+\frac{c^2-1}{2c}b\;>\;  b 
$$
Then there exists $n_0$ such that for all $n\geq n_0$ \eqref{eq:rateconvgsm} holds. For convenience, denote $d = \frac{1}{n {\beta_{\min}^*}_j} \ln (p_j / s_j-1)$ such that $\tau^*_j= {\beta_{\min}^*}_j/2 + d$. Then
$$e^{-\frac{n}{2}\left({\tau^*_j}^2-\frac{2\ln(p_j-s_j)}{n}\right)}
=e^{-\left[\frac{n}{8}{\beta_{\min}^{\star}}_j^2+\frac{n}{2}d^2 + \frac{1}{2}\left(\ln (p_j- s_j)-\ln (s_j)\right) -\ln (p_j- s_j)\right]}$$
Then, by considering separately the two posssible maximum in $\max\{\ln(p_j-s_j),\ln(s_j)\}$, we get that:
$$\frac{e^{-\frac{n}{2}\left({\tau^*_j}^2-\frac{2\ln(p_j-s_j)}{n}\right)}}{e^{-[\frac{n}{8}{{\beta_{\min}^*}_j}^2-\max\{\ln(p_j-s_j),\ln(s_j)\}]}}= 
e^{-\left[\frac{n}{2}d^2 + \frac{1}{2}\left|\ln (p_j- s_j)-\ln (s_j)\right|\right]} <1$$
The bound in \eqref{eq:optimrateconvgsm} follows from noting that $e^{-\tfrac{n}{2}\big[{\tau_j^*}^2-\tfrac{2\ln(p_j-s_j)}{n}\big]}=e^{-\tfrac{n}{2}\big[(\beta^\star_{\min,j}-{\tau_j^*})^2-\tfrac{2\ln(s_j)}{n}\big]}$ and summing over $j$.


\section{Proofs of Section~\ref{sec:properties_legression}}
\subsection{Proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:useful-reformulation}}
	First note that by discarding constant term 
 $$\arg\max_{M\in \mathcal{M}} \left\{\max_{\beta\in \mathcal{L}_M}\ell(\bs y; \bs \beta)-\sum_{j=1}^b\kappa_j |M_j| \right\}=\arg\min_{M\in \mathcal{M}} \left\{\min_{\beta\in \mathcal{L}_M}\bigg\{ \tfrac{1}{2}\|\bs y-\bs X\bs\beta\|^2\right\}+\sum_{j=1}^b\kappa_j |M_j| \bigg\}$$
 
 We also have $\min_{\beta\in \mathcal{L}_M} \tfrac{1}{2}\|\bs y-\bs X\bs\beta\|^2=\tfrac{1}{2}\|\bs y-\bs X_{\!\!M}\tilde{\bs \beta}^{(M)}\|^2=\tfrac{1}{2}\|\bs y\|^2-\tfrac{1}{2}\|\bs X_{\!\!M}\tilde{\bs\beta}^{(M)}\|^2$. The minimization in \eqref{eq:Mhat} can be then replaced with maximization of $\tfrac{1}{2}
 \|\bs X_{\!\!M}\tilde{\bs\beta}^{(M)}\|^2-\sum_{j=1}^b \kappa_j|M_j|$.

\subsection{Proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:L0toL1convergence}}
The first statement follows directly from Lemma~\ref{lem:L0toL1convergencegen} by taking $\{M_1,\ldots,M_k\}=\{S\}$. For the second statement, note that  $NC(M)=(1+\sum_{N \neq M} e^{C(N)}/ e^{C(M)})^{-1}$. Since for any $M,N\in \mathcal{M}$ and $M\neq N$, $C(N) / C(M)>0$, $NC(M) \leq (1+e^{C(M')-C(M)})^{-1}$.

\subsection{Proof of Lemma~\ref{lemma:s7}}
    This result follows directly from Lemma~S7 in \cite{rossell2022concentration} taking $\phi^*=1$.

\subsection{Proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:boundrho}}
This result follows directly Lemma~\ref{lem:boundrhogen} by taking $T=S$, and observing that $\min_{i\in S_j\setminus M_j}{\beta^*_i}^2\geq {\beta^*_{\min,j}}^2$.

\subsection{Proof of Theorem~\ref{theo:suffcondlinearmodel}} The result follows from Lemma~\ref{lem:boundexpnc}. In order to apply the Lemma, we want to show that $A_S$ is greater than $\tfrac{3}{4}|M\setminus S|$. Observe that $\Delta_{MS}$, defined in \eqref{eq:DeltaM}, can be rewritten as $\Delta_{MS}=\sum_{j=1}^b(|M_j\setminus S_j|-|S_j\setminus M_j|)\kappa_j$. By  Lemma~\ref{lem:boundrho}, for every $n\in \N$ we have 
\begin{equation}\label{eq:nonoverfittingcond}
   A_S\;=\; \gamma \Delta_{MS}+\frac{1-\gamma}{8}\mu_{Q_SM}\;\geq\; \gamma\sum_{j=1}^b|M_j \setminus S_j|\kappa_j + \sum_{j=1}^b |S_j\setminus M_j |\left(\tfrac{1-\gamma}{8} n \rho(\bs X) {\beta^{\star}_{\min,j}}^{2} - \gamma\kappa_j\right) 
\end{equation} 
Note that the right-hand side depends on $S$ and $M$ only through $|S_j\setminus M_j|$ and $|M_j\setminus S_j|$.  Observe that, by A6, $\gamma\sum_{j}|M_j\setminus S_j|\kappa_j\geq \tfrac{3}{4}|M\setminus S|$ as long as $\min_j \ln(p_j-s_j)\geq \tfrac32$, which always holds in our asymptotic regime for $n$ sufficiently large. Thus, it is enough to show that the last term in  \eqref{eq:nonoverfittingcond} is nonnegative for sufficiently large $n$, which follows directly from A7. For every $M\in \mathcal M\setminus \{S\}$, Lemma~\ref{lem:boundexpnc} gives the bound
$$
\E(NC(M))\;\leq\;16 e^{-A_S}A_S^{|M\setminus S|/2+1}+e^{-A_S},
$$
where $A=\gamma\Delta_{MS}+\tfrac{1-\gamma}{8}\mu_{Q_SM}$. A direct check verifies that the function $16 e^{-x}x^{\tfrac12|M\setminus S|+1}$ is decreasing for all $x>\tfrac12|M\setminus S|+1$ and so also the function $16 e^{-x}x^{\tfrac12|M\setminus S|+1}+e^{-x}$ is. Let 
$$A_S^*\;:=\;\gamma\sum_{j=1}^b|M_j \setminus S_j|\kappa_j + \sum_{j=1}^b |S_j\setminus M_j |\left(\tfrac{1-\gamma}{8} n \rho(\bs X) {\beta^{\star}_{\min,j}}^{2} - \gamma\kappa_j\right) $$
be the right hand side in \eqref{eq:nonoverfittingcond}. We showed that $A_S\geq A_S^*>\tfrac12|M\setminus S|+1$ and so we get 
$$
\E(NC(M))\;\leq\;16 e^{-A_S^*}{A_S^*}^{|M\setminus S|/2+1}+e^{-A_S^*}.
$$
Note that:
$$
    e^{-A_S^*}{A_S^*}^{|M\setminus S|/2+1} 
    =e^{-A_S^*\big[1-\big(\frac{|M\setminus S|}{2}+1\big)\frac{\ln(A_S^*)}{A_S^*}\big]}
$$
The function $\big(\frac{|M\setminus S|}{2}+1\big)\frac{\ln(A_S^*)}{A_S^*}$ is increasing in $|M\backslash S|$ and $\frac{\ln(A_S^*)}{A_S^*}$ is decreasing in $|S\backslash M|$. By setting $|M\setminus S|$ to $p-s$ and $|S\backslash M|$ to 0, for any $M\in \mathcal{M}$ we have the bound:
\begin{align*}
\Big(\frac{|M\setminus S|}{2}+1\Big)\frac{\ln(A_S^*)}{A_S^*} 
&\leq \Big(\frac{p-s}{2}+1\Big)\frac{\ln\big(\gamma\sum_{j=1}^b(p_j-s_j)\kappa_j\big)}{\gamma\sum_{j=1}^b(p_j-s_j)\kappa_j} \\
&\leq \Big(\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{p-s}\Big)\bigg(\frac{\ln(p-s)}{\gamma\sum_{j=1}^bq_j\kappa_j}+\frac{\ln\big(\gamma\sum_{j=1}^bq_j\kappa_j\big)}{\gamma\sum_{j=1}^bq_j\kappa_j}\Bigg)
\end{align*}
where $\bs q$ is the vector in $[0,1]^b$ such that  $q_j=\frac{p_j-s_j}{p-s}$ for all $j=1,\ldots,b$. We have $\sum_{j=1}^b q_j=1$ and by assumption~A6, $\kappa_j$ goes to infinity, then the component $\frac{\ln\big(\gamma\sum_{j=1}^bq_j\kappa_j\big)}{\gamma\sum_{j=1}^bq_j\kappa_j}$ goes to 0 as $n$ grows. The factor $1/(p-s)$ also goes to 0 by assumption~A2 and we have:
$$\Big(\frac{|M\setminus S|}{2}+1\Big)\frac{\ln(A_S^*)}{A_S^*} 
\leq \frac{1}{2}\frac{\ln(p-s)}{\gamma\sum_{j=1}^bq_j\kappa_j} + o(1)$$

By assumption~A6, we have:
$$
\gamma\sum_{j=1}^bq_j\kappa_j
=\gamma\sum_{j=1}^bq_j\ln(p_j-s_j) + \gamma b_n
=\gamma\sum_{j=1}^bq_j\ln(q_j)+ \gamma \ln(p-s) + \gamma b_n
$$
where the second equality follows from dividing and multiplying each $p_j-s_j$ by $p-s$. Dividing by $\ln(p-s)/2$, by the definition of $\gamma$ we get:
\begin{align*}
    \frac{2\gamma\sum_{j=1}^bq_j\kappa_j}{\ln(p-s)}
&=\frac{\gamma\sum_{j=1}^bq_j\ln(q_j)}{\ln(p-s)} + 2\gamma \Big(1+\frac{b_n}{\ln(p-s)}\Big) \\
&=  \frac{2\gamma }{2\gamma -1}-a
\end{align*}
where $a=\frac{-2\gamma\sum_{j=1}^bq_j\ln(q_j)}{\ln(p-s)}$. We then have
$$1-\Big(\frac{|M\setminus S|}{2}+1\Big)\frac{\ln(A_S^*)}{A_S^*} 
\geq 1- \frac{1}{\frac{2\gamma }{2\gamma -1}-a} + o(1) = \frac{1}{2\gamma}- \frac{a(2\gamma -1)}{2\gamma(\frac{2\gamma }{2\gamma -1}-a)} + o(1)$$
The numerator in $a$ is the entropy of distribution $\bs q$. We then have $|a|\leq \ln(b)/\ln(p-s)$ and the term in $a$ in the right-most expression above vanishes as $n$ grows. Since $\gamma\in(1/2,1)$, the limit of the lower bound above is at least $1/2$. 
% Note that for $x$ sufficiently large and any $\psi \in (0,1)$
% \begin{equation}\label{eq:boundexp}
% 16e^{-x}x^{\tfrac12|M\setminus S|+1}+e^{-x}\leq e^{-\psi x}.    
% \end{equation}

% By assumption~A6 $\kappa_j$ goes to infinity. Assumption~A7 also implies there exist $c^{\prime}_j \to +\infty$ such that:
% $$\frac{(1-\gamma) n\rho(\bs X)}{8}{{\beta_{\min}^{\star}}_j}^2 \,- \,\kappa_j \;=\; \ln(s_j) + c^{\prime}_{j}.$$
% Then $A_S^*$ goes to infinity and for $n$ sufficiently large and 
We then have for any $\psi \in(0,1/2)$ and $n$ large enough: 
$$
\E(NC(M))\;\leq\;e^{-\psi A_S^*}.
$$
Let $\delta \in (0,1)$ and denote $\bar{m}_j=\max\big\{\frac{2\ln(p_j-s_j)}{b_{j}},\frac{2\ln(s_j)}{c^{\prime}_{j}}\big\}$. Take $\psi=\max_{j=1,\ldots,b}\frac{\xi + \delta + \bar{m}_j}{1+\bar{m}_j}$ for some $\xi \in(0, 1- \delta)$ then $\psi \in (0,1/2)$ and we have for every $j=1,\ldots,b$:
$$\delta \leq \psi-(1-\psi)\bar{m}_j.$$ 
By definition of $\gamma$ we have: 
$$
\gamma\kappa_j\;\geq\;\frac12 (1+\alpha_j)\kappa_j\;=\;\ln(p_j-s_j)+\frac12(\kappa_j-\ln(p_j-s_j)) \;=\; \ln(p_j-s_j)+ \frac12b_{j}.
$$
From our choice of $\psi,\delta$, we get:
$$\psi\gamma\kappa_j \;\geq\; \psi\big(\ln(p_j-s_j)+ \frac12b_{j}\big) \;\geq\;  \ln(p_j-s_j)+ \delta\frac12b_{j}.$$
By A7 and by our choice of $\psi,\delta$,
$$\psi\left(\tfrac{1-\gamma}{8} n \rho(\bs X) {\beta^{\star}_{\min,j}}^{2} - \gamma\kappa_j\right) \;\geq\; \psi\left(\ln(s_j)+c_j'\right) \;\geq\; \ln(s_j)+\delta \frac{c^{\prime}_j}{2}.
$$% \paul{\@Piotr your had:
% Define $\beta_j=\tfrac{\ln(s_j)}{\tfrac{1-\gamma}{8}n\rho(\bs X){\beta_{\min,j}^\star}^2-\kappa_j}$. Positivity of $\beta_j$ follows immediately. To get an upper bound for $\beta_j$, note that, by A7, 
% $$
% \tfrac{\ln(s_j)}{\tfrac{1-\gamma}{8}n\rho(\bs X){\beta_{\min,j}^\star}^2-\kappa_j}\;\leq \;\tfrac{\ln(s_j)}{\ln(s_j)+2\sqrt{\kappa_j\ln(s_j)}}\;= \;\tfrac{1}{1+2\sqrt{\tfrac{\kappa_j}{\ln(s_j)}}}
% $$
% % \piotr{Add a suitable assumption to bound it away from 1 (e.g. $b^2\kappa_j>\ln s_j$ for some $b\in \R$ and $n$ sufficiently large). This still requires correction.}
% We follow similar arguments as above to argue that
% $$
% (1+\beta_j)(\tfrac{1-\gamma}{8}n\rho(\bs X){\beta_{\min,j}^\star}^2-\kappa_j)\;=\;(1+\frac{1}{\beta_j})\ln(s_j)
% $$
% concluding that 
% $$
% \tfrac{1-\gamma}{8}n\rho(\bs X){\beta_{\min,j}^\star}^2-\kappa_j\;\geq \;c' \ln(s_j),\qquad c'=1+\tfrac{2}{b}<\min_j \frac{1+\frac{1}{\beta_j}}{1+\beta_j}=\min_j \frac{1}{\beta_j}.
% $$
% We then also have $\tfrac{1-\gamma}{8}n\rho(\bs X){\beta_{\min,j}^\star}^2-\gamma\kappa_j>c'\ln(s_j)$.\\
% Since $(1+\alpha_j)\kappa_j
% =(1+\tfrac{1}{\alpha_j})\ln(p_j-s_j)$ by the definition of $\alpha_j$, and $\gamma\in (\tfrac12,\tfrac{1+a}{2})$, we get 
% $$
% \gamma\kappa_j\;>\;\frac12 (1+\tfrac{1}{\alpha_j})\ln(p_j-s_j)\;=\;(1+\tfrac12(\tfrac{1}{\alpha_j}-1))\ln(p_j-s_j)\;\geq\;c\ln(p_j-s_j).
% $$
% where 
% $$
% c\;=\;\tfrac{1+a}{2a}\;>\;1.
% $$}

% \paul{Piotr, you had,  Take any $\delta\in (\frac{1}{\min\{c,c'\}},1)$ so that $\delta c, \delta c'>1$. 
% Thus if $A^*$ is sufficiently large, we get
% $$
% \E(NC(M))\;\leq\;\exp\{-\delta \sum_{j=1}^b |M_j\setminus S_j|\ln(p_j-s_j)-\delta c'\sum_{j=1}^b |S_j\setminus M_j|\ln(s_j)\}.
% $$} \\
Using the expression for $A^*$, the bound in \eqref{eq:boundexp}, and the above inequalities, we get
$$
\E(NC(M))\;\leq\;\exp\{- \sum_{j=1}^b |M_j\setminus S_j|(\ln(p_j-s_j)+\delta \tfrac{b_j}{2})-\sum_{j=1}^b |S_j\setminus M_j|(\ln(s_j)+\delta \tfrac{c^{\prime}_j}{2})\}.
$$





Denote $\mathcal{S}= \sum_{M \in \mathcal{M}\setminus \{S\}} \E\left(NC(M)\right)$ for convenience. We can split $\mathcal{S}$ into sums over the models that have the same number of spurious variables and same number of missing variables in every block, that is the models $M$ such that for all $j$, $|M_j\setminus S_j|=u_j$ and $|S_j\setminus M_j|=w_j$ with $u_j \in \{0,\ldots,p_j-s_j\}$ and $w_j \in \{0,\ldots,s_j\}$, not all simultaneously zero:
$$\mathcal{S} =\sum_{w_1=0}^{s_1}\cdots\sum_{w_b=0}^{s_b}\sum_{u_1=0}^{p_1-s_1}\cdots\sum_{u_b=0}^{p_b-s_b}\sum_{M \neq S:\forall \,j |M_j\setminus S_j|=u_j, |S_j\setminus M_j|=w_j } \E\left(NC(M)\right)$$
The number of models with given collection of $u_j$'s and $w_j$'s is $\prod_{j=1}^b {p_j-s_j \choose u_j}{s_j \choose w_j}$. We thus have:
\begin{equation*}
    \mathcal{S} \leq \prod_{j=1}^b\left(1+\sum_{u_j=1}^{p_j-s_j}{p_j-s_j \choose u_j}e^{- u_j(\ln(p_j-s_j)+\delta \tfrac{b_j}{2})}\right)\left(1+
    \sum_{w_j=1}^{s_j}{s_j \choose w_j}
    e^{-w_j(\ln(s_j)+\delta \tfrac{c^{\prime}_j}{2})}\right) - 1
\end{equation*}
where $-1$ stems from discarding $S$ from the product-sum, which corresponds to the case $u_j=0$ and $w_j=0$ for all $j$. A standard bound on binomial coefficient for $1\leq k\leq n$ is:
\begin{equation*}
    {n \choose k} \leq \left(\frac{n\,e}{k}\right)^k \leq \left(n\,e\right)^k
\end{equation*}
Then
\begin{equation*}
    \mathcal{S} \;\;\leq\;\; \prod_{j=1}^b\left(1+\sum_{u_j=1}^{p_j-s_j}e^{-u_j\left(\delta \tfrac{b_j}{2}-1\right)}\right)\left(1+
    \sum_{w_j=1}^{s_j}    e^{-w_j\left(\delta \tfrac{c^{\prime}_j}{2}-1\right)}\right) - 1
\end{equation*}
Denote
$$
g_j\;=\;e^{1-\delta \tfrac{b_j}{2}},\qquad h_j\;=\;e^{1-\delta \tfrac{c^{\prime}_j}{2}}
$$
where both expressions go to zero as $n$ increases.
For every $j$, we have:
\begin{align*}
    &1+\sum_{u_j=1}^{p_j-s_j}e^{-u_j\left(\delta \tfrac{b_j}{2}-1\right)}
    =\frac{1-g_j^{p_j-s_j+1}}{1-g_j} \\
    &1+\sum_{w_j=1}^{s_j}    e^{-w_j\left(\delta \tfrac{c^{\prime}_j}{2}-1\right)} =\frac{1-h_j^{s_j+1}}{1-h_j}.
\end{align*}
Since both expressions converge to $1$ as $n$ grows, we conclude $\mathcal S\to 0$.

\subsection{Proof of Theorem~\ref{prop:fullrecovreg}}
From the proof of Theorem~\ref{theo:suffcondlinearmodel} we have:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:prodconstreg}
    \mathcal{S} \;\;\leq\;\; \prod_{j=1}^b\left(1+\sum_{u_j=1}^{p_j-s_j}e^{-u_j\left(\delta \tfrac{b_j}{2}-1\right)}\right)\left(1+
    \sum_{w_j=1}^{s_j}    e^{-w_j\left(\delta \tfrac{c^{\prime}_j}{2}-1\right)}\right) - 1
\end{equation}
For every $j$, we have:
\begin{align*}
    &\sum_{u_j=1}^{p_j-s_j}e^{-u_j\left(\delta \tfrac{b_j}{2}-1\right)}
    =g_j\,\frac{1-g_j^{p_j-s_j}}{1-g_j} \\
    &\sum_{w_j=1}^{s_j}    e^{-w_j\left(\delta \tfrac{c^{\prime}_j}{2}-1\right)} =h_j\,\frac{1-h_j^{s_j}}{1-h_j}.
\end{align*}
Each of the sum above is smaller than 1 for $n$ sufficiently large. Then every term of degree larger than 1 in the product in \eqref{eq:prodconstreg} is bounded above by the sum of terms of degree 1. For $n$ large enough, we get: 
 \begin{equation*} \mathcal{S} \leq (2^{2b}-2b)\sum_{j=1}^b \bigg[g_j\,\frac{1-g_j^{p_j-s_j}}{1-g_j}+ h_j\,\frac{1-h_j^{s_j}}{1-h_j}\bigg ]. \end{equation*} 
 Take $$r_b=\max_{j=1,\ldots,b}\bigg\{\frac{1-g_j^{p_j-s_j}}{1-g_j} \,,\,\frac{1-h_j^{s_j}}{1-h_j} \bigg\}.$$
 By Lemma~\ref{lem:L0toL1convergence} and observing that $2e \leq 6$, we get: 
 $$ P(\hat{S}^{b} \neq S)\;\leq  2 \sum_{M \in \mathcal{M}\setminus \{S\}} \E\left(NC(M)\right) \;\leq\ (2^{2b}-2b)6r_b\,\,\sum_{j=1}^b\,\, e^{-\tfrac{\delta}{2}\big[\kappa_j-\ln(p_j-s_j)\big]} + e^{-\tfrac{\delta}{2}\big[\frac{(1-\gamma) n\rho(\bs X)}{8}{{\beta_{\min}^{\star}}_j}^2 - \kappa_j -\ln(s_j) \big]} $$ and 
 $$ P(\hat{S}^{b} \neq S)\;\leq \;(2^{2b}-2b)6r_b\sum_{j=1}^b e^{-\tfrac{\delta}{2}\big[\kappa_j-\ln(p_j-s_j)\big]} + e^{-\frac{\delta}{2}\big[(\sqrt{\frac{(1-\gamma) n\rho(\bs X)}{8}}{{\beta_{\min}^{\star}}_j} - \sqrt{\kappa_j})^2 -\ln(s_j) \big]}.$$
     The bound in \eqref{eq:oraclerateconvreg} follows from proceeding as in the proof of Theorem~\ref{theo:fullrecovGS} with $c=1+\varepsilon$ such that $\lim \varepsilon > 0$. Under \eqref{betamincond:minimalreg}, for all $n\geq n_0$  we have $\sqrt{\kappa^*_j} \geq  (\frac{\varepsilon^2}{2(1+\varepsilon)}+1)\sqrt{\ln(p_j-s_j)}$ and $\frac{\sqrt{(1-\gamma) n\rho(\bs X)}}{4}{{\beta_{\min}^{\star}}_j}-\sqrt{\kappa^*} \geq (\frac{\varepsilon^2}{2(1+\varepsilon)}+1)\sqrt{\ln(s_j)}$
    which implies A6 and A7. Then there exists $r_b\to 1$ and $n_0$ such that for all $n\geq n_0$ \eqref{eq:rateconvreg} holds and proceeding as in the proof of Theorem~\ref{theo:fullrecovGS} shows that for any $j$ 
    $$e^{-\frac{\delta}{2}\left[{\kappa^*_j}-\ln(p_j-s_j)\right]}+e^{-\frac{\delta}{2}\big[(\sqrt{\frac{(1-\gamma) n\rho(\bs X)}{8}}{{\beta_{\min}^{\star}}_j} - \sqrt{\kappa_j^*})^2 -\ln(s_j) \big]} <2 e^{-\frac{\delta}{2}[\frac{(1-\gamma) n\rho(\bs X)}{32}{{\beta_{\min}^*}_j}^2-\max\{\ln(p_j-s_j),\ln(s_j)\}]}. $$
%For convenience, denote $d=\frac{\sqrt{2(1-\gamma) n\rho(\bs X)}}{4}$ and $\phi = \frac{2}{n 2(1-\gamma) \rho(\bs X){\beta_{\min}^*}_j} \ln (p_j / s_j-1)$ such that ${\kappa^\star_j\,}^{\frac 1 2}= d {\beta_{\min}^*}_j/2 + \phi$. Then
% $$e^{-\frac{\delta}{2}\left[{\kappa^*_j}-\ln(p_j-s_j)\right]}
% =e^{-\frac{\delta}{2}\left[\frac{d^2}{4}{\beta_{\min}^{\star}}_j^2+\phi^2 + \frac{1}{2}\left(\ln (p_j- s_j)-\ln (s_j)\right) -\ln (p_j- s_j)\right]}$$
% Then, by considering separately the cases $\max\{\ln(p_j-s_j),\ln(s_j)\}=\ln(p_j-s_j)$ and $\max\{\ln(p_j-s_j),\ln(s_j)\}=\ln(s_j)$, we get that:
% $$\frac{e^{-\frac{\delta}{2}\left[{\kappa^*_j}-\ln(p_j-s_j)\right]}}
% {e^{-\frac{\delta}{2}[\frac{d^2}{4}{{\beta_{\min}^*}_j}^2-\max\{\ln(p_j-s_j),\ln(s_j)\}]}} = 
% e^{-\frac{\delta}{2}\left[\phi^2 + \frac{1}{2}\left|\ln (p_j- s_j)-\ln (s_j)\right|\right]} <1$$
% The bound in \eqref{eq:optimrateconvreg} follows from noting that $e^{-\tfrac{\delta}{2}\big[\kappa_j^*-\ln(p_j-s_j)\big]}= 
%     e^{-\frac{\delta}{2}\big[(\frac{\sqrt{2(1-\gamma) n\rho(\bs X)}}{4}{{\beta_{\min}^{\star}}_j} - \sqrt{\kappa_j^*})^2 -\ln(s_j) \big]} $ and summing the over $j$.

\subsection{Proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:L0toL1convergence2}} Take $\{M_1,\ldots,M_k\}=\mathcal{T}$ in Lemma~\ref{lem:L0toL1convergencegen}, then:
$$
P(\hat{S}^b\not\in \mathcal{T})\;\leq\; (|\mathcal{T}|+1) \sum_{M \in \mathcal{M}\setminus \{S\}}\E\left( NC(M)\right)$$
The result follows from observing that $|\mathcal{T}|=|\mathcal{P}(S^I)|=2^{|S^I|}$.


\subsection{Proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:nectypeIreg}}

Recovery of $S$ requires that for every $M\in O_j$, $NC(M)/NC(S)<1$, then it requires $\max_{M\in O_j}\frac{NC(M)}{NC(S)}<1$. By the definition of the normalized criterion $NC$:
$$P(\hat{S}^b=S) \leq P\Big(\max_{M\in O_j}\frac{NC(M)}{NC(S)}<1\Big)\leq  P\big(\max_{M\in O_j}\sqrt{L_{MS}}<\sqrt{2\kappa_j}\Big)$$
By Lemma~\ref{lemma:s7}, for every $M\in O_j$, $L_{MS}\in \chi^2_1$ and there exists $Z_M\sim N(0,1)$ such that $\sqrt{L_{MS}}=|Z_M|$. Since for every $M\in O_j$, $|Z_M|\geq Z_M$, we have:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:upperboundrecov}
    P(\hat{S}^b=S) \leq  1-P\big(\max_{M\in O_j}Z_M\geq\sqrt{2\kappa_j}\Big)
\end{equation}

The set $O_j$ has cardinality $p_j-s_j$, then by Theorem 3.4 in \cite{hartigan} and assumption A2, for any $\epsilon>0$:
$$P\big(\max_{M\in O_j}Z_M \geq \underline{\lambda}_j \sqrt{2 \ln(p_j-s_j)}(1-\epsilon)\big) \to 1$$
If $\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{\kappa_j}{\underline{\lambda}_j^2\ln(p_j-s_j)} <1$, then $P\big(\max_{M\in O_j}Z_M\geq\sqrt{2\kappa_j}\big) \to 1$ and by \eqref{eq:upperboundrecov} $P(\hat{S}^b=S) \to 0$.

\subsection{Proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:upperboundnoncentral}}
The result follows directly from Lemma~\ref{lem:upperboundnoncentralgen}, with $M=S$ since $Q_T=S$.

\subsection{Proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:nectypeIIreg}}

Let $M=S\setminus S^S$, since $S^S\neq \emptyset$, $M\neq S$. The event $(\hat{S}^b=S)$ implies $\big(\frac{NC(M)}{NC(S)}<1\big)$ then $P(\hat{S}^b=S)\leq P\big(\frac{NC(M)}{NC(S)}<1\big)$. By definition of $NC$, we have
$P\big(\frac{NC(M)}{NC(S)}<1\big) = P(L_{SM}> 2\Delta_{SM})$ where by Lemma~\ref{lemma:s7},  $L_{SM}\sim \chi_{|S\setminus M|}^2(\mu_{SM})$. We  bound next $P(L_{SM}> 2\Delta_{SM})$ with Lemma~\ref{lemma:s1s3}. We first need to check that $2\Delta_{SM} > \mu_{SM} + |S\setminus M|$.
On one side, by Lemma~\ref{lem:upperboundnoncentral} we have:
$\mu_{SM} + |S\setminus M| \leq  \sum_{j=1}^b |S_j\setminus M_j|(n\bar{\lambda}\max_{i\in S_j\setminus M_j}{\beta_i^*}^2+1)$. On the other side, $2\Delta_{SM}=\sum_{j=1}^b |S_j\setminus M_j|2 \kappa_j$. Observe that $S\setminus M \subset S^S$, then by definition of $S^S$ and since $\kappa_j \to +\infty$ for all $j=1,\ldots,n$, for $n$ large enough, $2 \kappa_j >(n\bar{\lambda}\max_{i\in S_j\setminus M_j}|\beta_i^*|+1)$. We then have $2\Delta_{SM} > \mu_{SM} + |S\setminus M|$ and by Lemma~\ref{lemma:s1s3}:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:randomeqeee}
    P(L_{SM}> 2\Delta_{SM}) \leq e^{-\Delta_{SM}\left(1-\sqrt{\frac{\mu_{SM}}{2\Delta_{SM}}}\right)^2}\left(\frac{2\Delta_{SM}}{\mu_{SM}}\right)^{\frac{|S\setminus M|}{4}}
\end{equation}
By Lemma~\ref{lem:upperboundnoncentral} :
$$\frac{\mu_{SM}}{2\Delta_{SM}}\leq \frac{ \sum_{j=1}^b |S_j\setminus M_j|n\bar{\lambda}\max_{i\in S_j\setminus M_j}{\beta_i^*}^2}{\sum_{j=1}^b |S_j\setminus M_j|2 \kappa_j}$$
Observe that:
\begin{eqnarray*}
    \sum_{j=1}^b\frac{n\bar{\lambda}\max_{i\in S_j\setminus M_j}{\beta_i^{\star}}^2}{2\kappa_j}
    &\geq &\sum_{j=1,|S_j\setminus M_j|\neq 0}^b\frac{|S_j\setminus M_j|n\bar{\lambda}\max_{i\in S_j\setminus M_j}{\beta_i^{\star}}^2}{|S_j\setminus M_j|2\kappa_j} \\
    &=& \frac{\sum_{j=1,|S_j\setminus M_j|\neq 0 }^b|S_j\setminus M_j|n\bar{\lambda}\max_{i\in S_j\setminus M_j}{\beta_i^{\star}}^2\Big(1+\sum_{l\neq j}\frac{|S_l\setminus M_l|2\kappa_l}{|S_j\setminus M_j|2\kappa_j}\Big)}{\sum_{j=1}^b|S_j\setminus M_j|2\kappa_j} \\
    &\geq & \frac{\sum_{j=1}^b|S_j\setminus M_j|n\bar{\lambda}\max_{i\in S_j\setminus M_j}{\beta_i^{\star}}^2}{\sum_{j=1}^b \big|S\setminus M_j\big|2\kappa_j} 
\end{eqnarray*}
where last inequality follows from $\Big(1+\sum_{l\neq j}\frac{|S_l^S|\kappa_l}{|S_j^S|\kappa_j}\Big)\geq 1$ for all $j$ such that $|S_j^S|\neq 0$. We have $S_j\setminus M_j \subset S_j^S$ for every $j=1,\ldots,b$, then by definition of the $S_j^S$, $\sum_{j=1}^b\frac{n\bar{\lambda}\max_{i\in S_j\setminus M_j}{\beta_i^{\star}}^2}{2\kappa_j}$ goes to 0 and so does $\frac{\mu_{SM}}{2\Delta_{SM}}$. By \eqref{eq:randomeqeee}, for any $\phi \in (0,1)$, for $n$ large enough:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:randomeqeee}
    P(L_{SM}> 2\Delta_{SM}) \leq e^{-\phi\Delta_{SM}}\left(\frac{2\Delta_{SM}}{\mu_{SM}}\right)^{\frac{|S\setminus M|}{4}}
\end{equation} 
By Lemma~\ref{lemma:s7}, for $n$ large enough $\mu_{SM} \geq 2$, then:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:randomeqeee}
    P(L_{SM}> 2\Delta_{SM}) \leq e^{-\phi\Delta_{SM}}\left(\Delta_{SM}\right)^{\frac{|S\setminus M|}{4}}
\end{equation} 
and for any $\alpha\in(0,1)$, for $n$ large enough, we have:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:randomeqeee}
    P(L_{SM}> 2\Delta_{SM}) \leq e^{-\alpha\phi\Delta_{SM}}
\end{equation} 
where the right hand-side goes to 0 since for all $j=1,\ldots,b$, $\kappa_j \to +\infty$. 

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------

The result follows from in Lemma~\ref{lem:boundexpnc2}. Take $T=S^L\cup S^I$ and $M=S$ . Since $S^S\neq \emptyset$, $M\neq T$ and we do have $M\cap S^I=S^I=T\cap S^I$ and $M\setminus (T\cap S^I)=S^S\neq \emptyset$. In addition, $Q_{T}=S=M$ then $L_{Q_TM}=0$ and $\mu_{Q_TM}=0$. Since $|M\setminus T|=|S^S|$, by Lemma~\ref{lemma:s7},  $L_{Q_TT}\sim \chi_{|S^S|}^2(\mu_{Q_TT})$ with:
$$
\mu_{Q_TT}= \|\big(I_n-P_S\big) \bs X_{S^S} \bs \beta^{\star}_{S^S}\|^2 = n {\beta^{\star}_{S^S}}^\top \Big(\frac1n \bs X_{S^S}^\top(I_n-P_S)\bs X_{S^S}\Big)\beta^{\star}_{S^S}$$
We have the following bounds on $\mu_{Q_TT}$
\begin{eqnarray*}
  n\bar{\lambda}\|\beta^{\star}_{S^S}\|^2 &\geq \mu_{Q_TT} &\geq  n\bar{\lambda}\|\beta^{\star}_{S^S}\|^2 \\
    n\bar{\lambda}\sum_{j=1}^b|S_j^S|\max_{i\in S_j^S}{\beta_i^{\star}}^2  &\geq  \mu_{Q_TT} \geq & n\lambda_{min}\Big(\frac1n \bs X_{S^S}^\top(I_n-P_S)\bs X_{S^S}\Big)\sum_{j=1}^b|S_j^S|\min_{i\in S_j^S}{\beta_i^{\star}}^2
\end{eqnarray*}
where $\bar{\lambda}$ was defined in \eqref{lem:upperboundnoncentral} and the last inequalitis follows from the bounds $\|\beta^{\star}_{S^S}\|^2 \geq \sum_{j=1}^b|S_j^S|\min_{i\in S_j^S}{\beta_i^{\star}}^2$ and $\|\beta^{\star}_{S^S}\|^2\leq \sum_{j=1}^b|S_j^S|\max_{i\in S_j^S}{\beta_i^{\star}}^2$. 
For $\bs X_S$ full rank, $\Big(\frac1n \bs X_{S^S}^\top(I_n-P_S)\bs X_{S^S}\Big)$ is bounded away from 0 and for $n$ large enough $\mu_{Q_TT}\geq 1$.

Observe that $A_T\,=\,\gamma\sum_{j=1}^b \kappa_j|S^S_j|$ for some $\gamma\in(1/2,1]$ and then:
$$\frac{\sum_{j=1}^b|S_j^S|n\bar{\lambda}\max_{i\in S_j^S}{\beta_i^{\star}}^2}{\sum_{j=1}^b \big|S^S_j\big|\gamma\kappa_j}\geq \frac{\mu_{Q_TT}}{A_t} \geq 0$$
An upper bound on the left hand-side is:
\begin{eqnarray*}
    \sum_{j=1}^b\frac{n\bar{\lambda}\max_{i\in S_j^S}{\beta_i^{\star}}^2}{\gamma\kappa_j}
    &\geq &\sum_{j=1,|S_j^S|\neq 0}^b\frac{|S_j^S|n\bar{\lambda}\max_{i\in S_j^S}{\beta_i^{\star}}^2}{|S_j^S|\gamma\kappa_j} \\
    &=& \frac{\sum_{j=1,|S_j^S|\neq 0 }^b|S_j^S|n\bar{\lambda}\max_{i\in S_j^S}{\beta_i^{\star}}^2\Big(1+\sum_{l\neq j}\frac{|S_l^S|\kappa_l}{|S_j^S|\kappa_j}\Big)}{\sum_{j=1}^b|S_j^S|\gamma\kappa_j} \\
    &\geq & \frac{\sum_{j=1}^b|S_j^S|n\bar{\lambda}\max_{i\in S_j^S}{\beta_i^{\star}}^2}{\sum_{j=1}^b \big|S^S_j\big|\gamma\kappa_j} 
\end{eqnarray*}
where last inequality follows from $\Big(1+\sum_{l\neq j}\frac{|S_l^S|\kappa_l}{|S_j^S|\kappa_j}\Big)\geq 1$ for all $j$ such that $|S_j^S|\neq 0$. By definition of the $S_j^S$, $\sum_{j=1}^b\frac{n\bar{\lambda}\max_{i\in S_j^S}{\beta_i^{\star}}^2}{\gamma\kappa_j}$ goes to 0 and so does $\frac{\mu_{Q_TT}}{A_T}$. $\phi$ are can then be chosen arbitrarily close to 1 and for $n$ large enough: $\phi < \Big(1-\big(\frac{\mu_{Q_T T}}{2 \Lambda_T}\big)^{\frac{1}{3}}\Big)^2$. 

Since for all $j$ $\kappa_j \to +\infty$ for $n$ large enough, $\phi A_T \geq \max\{7,\frac{3}{4}|S^S|\}$ and by Lemma~\ref{lem:boundexpnc2}:
$$\E(NC(S))\leq 2e^{-\phi A_T}\big[2(\phi+1)A_T\big]^{\frac{|S^S|}{4}+1}+e^{-A_T}$$
For any $0<\alpha<\phi$, for $n$ large enough: 
$$2e^{-\phi A_T}\big[2(\phi+1)A_T\big]^{\frac{|S^S|}{4}+1}+e^{-A_T} \leq e^{-\alpha A_T}$$
Since $\phi$ can be taken arbitrarily close to 1, for any $\alpha\in(0,1)$, for $n$ large enough:
$$\E(NC(S))\leq e^{-\alpha\gamma \sum_{j=1}^b|S_j^S|\kappa_j}$$
where the right hand-side goes to 0 since for all $j$ $\kappa_j \to +\infty$. Then $NC(S)$ converges in probability to 0. 

Suppose $NC()$ while $\sum_{}$

\subsection{Proof of Corollary~\ref{cor:necrecovreg}}
If for some $j\in\{1,\ldots,b\}$ $\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{\sqrt{n\bar{\lambda}}\beta_{\min,j}^{\star}}{\sqrt{\underline{\lambda}_j\ln(p_j-s_j)}} = 0$ then we have:
$$\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{\sqrt{n\bar{\lambda}}\beta_{\min,j}^{\star}}{\sqrt{\kappa_j}}\sqrt{\frac{\kappa_j}{\underline{\lambda}_j\ln(p_j-s_j)}} = 0$$
If $\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{\sqrt{n\bar{\lambda}}\beta_{\min,j}^{\star}}{\sqrt{\kappa_j}}>0$, then $\lim_{n\to\infty}\sqrt{\frac{\kappa_j}{\underline{\lambda}_j\ln(p_j-s_j)}} = 0$ and by Proposition~\ref{prop:nectypeIreg} $P(\hat{S}^b=S)\not\to1$. If $\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{\sqrt{n\bar{\lambda}}\beta_{\min,j}^{\star}}{\sqrt{\kappa_j}}=0$, then by Proposition~\ref{prop:nectypeIIreg} $P(\hat{S}^b=S)\not\to1$.

\subsection{Proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:empiricalbayesinterpret}}
Denote $H(\bs \theta)=p(\bs y \mid \bs \theta)$. For any $j=1,\ldots,b$, the partial derivative with respect to $\theta^{(j)}$ is:
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:logderivative}
        \frac{\partial \ln H(\bs \theta)}{\partial \theta^{(j)}}=\frac{\partial H(\bs \theta)}{\partial \theta^{(j)}}\,H(\boldsymbol{\theta})^{-1}
    \end{equation}
    Observe that:
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:partialmarglikelihood}
    H(\bs \theta)=\sum_{M \in \mathcal{M}}p(\bs y \mid M)p(M \mid \bs \theta)
    \quad \text{and}\quad 
    \frac{\partial H(\bs \theta)}{\partial \theta^{(j)}}=\sum_{M\in \mathcal{M}}p(\bs y \mid M)\frac{\partial p(M \mid \bs \theta)}{\partial \theta^{(j)}}
    \end{equation}
    By our choice of prior~\eqref{eq:bernoulliprior}:
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:partialprobmodel}
    %p(M \mid \bs \theta)=\prod_{j=1}^b \theta^{(j)}^{\sum_{i\in B_j}m_i} \, (1-\theta^{(j)})^{p_j - \sum_{i\in B_j}m_i}\quad\text{and}\quad 
    \frac{\partial p(M \mid \bs \theta)}{\partial \theta^{(j)}}= p(M \mid \bs \theta) \bigg(\frac{\sum_{i\in B_j}m_i}{\theta^{(j)}}-\frac{p_j-\sum_{i\in B_j}m_i}{1-\theta^{(j)}} \bigg)        
    \end{equation}
    Replacing \eqref{eq:partialprobmodel} into \eqref{eq:partialmarglikelihood}, we get:
    \begin{equation*}\label{eq:partialmarglikelihood2}
        \frac{\partial H(\bs \theta)}{\partial \theta^{(j)}}=\frac{1}{\theta^{(j)}}\sum_{i \in B_j}\sum_{M\in \mathcal{M}: m_i=1}p(\bs y \mid M)p(M \mid \bs \theta) - \frac{1}{1-\theta^{(j)}}\bigg[p_j H(\bs \theta)-\sum_{i \in B_j}\sum_{M\in \mathcal{M}: m_i=1}p(\bs y \mid M)p(M \mid \bs \theta)
        \bigg]
    \end{equation*}
    Note that:
    $$\frac{\sum_{M\in \mathcal{M}: m_i=1}p(\bs y \mid M)p(M \mid \bs \theta)}{\sum_{M\in \mathcal{M}}p(\bs y \mid M)p(M \mid \bs \theta)} = P(m_i = 1\mid \bs y)$$
    By \eqref{eq:logderivative}, we then get the following expression of the partial derivative for any $j=1,\ldots,b$:
    $$\frac{\partial \ln H(\bs \theta)}{\partial \theta^{(j)}}=\frac{1}{\theta^{(j)}}\sum_{i \in B_j}P(m_i = 1\mid \bs y) - \frac{1}{1-\theta^{(j)}}\bigg[p_j-\sum_{i \in B_j}P(m_i = 1\mid \bs y)
        \bigg]$$
    Setting the partial derivatives to 0 and solving for $\theta^{(j)}$ gives the result.

For every $j=1,\ldots,b$, we have:
\begin{equation*}
    \frac{\widehat{s_j}}{p_j}
   = \frac{1}{p_j}\sum_{M \in \mathcal{M}}NC(M)\sum_{i \in B_j}I(i\in M) =\sum_{M \in \mathcal{M} | M \neq S}\frac{|M_j|}{p_j}NC(M) +\frac{s_j}{p_j}NC(S) 
\end{equation*}
Using that $NC(S)=1-\sum_{M \in \mathcal{M} | M \neq S}NC(M)$, we get
\begin{equation*}
   \frac{\widehat{s_j}}{p_j}
   =\sum_{M \in \mathcal{M} | M \neq S}\frac{|M_j|-s_j}{p_j}NC(M) \,\,+\,\, \frac{s_j}{p_j}
\end{equation*}
Splitting the sum between models $M$ where $|M_j|\geq s_j$ and models $M$ where $|M_j|<s_j$, we can write:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:rewriteshat}
  \frac{\widehat{s}_j}{p_j}  = \frac{s_j}{p_j}\,\,+\,\, A_j-\,\,B_j  
\end{equation}
where:
$$
    A_j = \sum_{M \in \mathcal{M} | |M_j| \geq s_j}\frac{|M_j|-s_j}{p_j}NC(M)\quad\text{and}\quad
    B_j = \sum_{M \in \mathcal{M} | |M_j| < s_j}\frac{s_j-|M_j|}{p_j}NC(M)
$$
For any $M_j$, such that $|M_j|\geq s_j$, $\frac{|M_j|-s_j}{p_j}\in [0,\frac{p_j-s_j}{p_j}]$ and since $\sum_{M \in \mathcal{M} | |M_j| \geq s_j}NC(M)\in [0,1]$, $A_j\in [0,\frac{p_j-s_j}{p_j}]$. Similarly, $B_j\in [0,\frac{s_j}{p_j}]$. As a result,
\begin{equation*}
 \frac{s_j}{p_j}-\frac{s_j}{p_j}\sum_{M \in \mathcal{M} | |M_j| < s_j}NC(M) \leq \frac{s_j}{p_j}-B_j \leq \frac{\widehat{s}_j}{p_j}  \leq \frac{s_j}{p_j}+ A_j \leq \frac{s_j}{p_j}+ \frac{p_j-s_j}{p_j}\sum_{M \in \mathcal{M} | |M_j| \geq s_j}NC(M)
\end{equation*}

\subsection{Proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:convtoT}}
By definition of $\mathcal{T}$, for any $M \not\in\mathcal{T}$, there exists $T\in\mathcal{T}$ such that $M\cap S^I=T\cap S^I$ and $M\neq T$. To apply Lemma~\ref{lem:boundexpnc2}, we need to check $\phi A_T\geq \max\{\tfrac{3}{4}|M\setminus S|,7\}$ for some $\phi\in(0,1)$ if $M\cap S^S=\emptyset$ or some $\phi\in\bigg(0,\Big(1-\big(\frac{\mu_{Q_T T}}{2 A_T}\big)^{\frac{1}{3}}\Big)^2\bigg)$ if $M\cap S^S\neq\emptyset$. Observe that $\Delta_{MT}$, defined in \eqref{eq:DeltaM}, can be rewritten as $\Delta_{MT}=\sum_{j=1}^b(|M_j\setminus T_j|-|T_j\setminus M_j|)\kappa_j$. By Lemma~\ref{lem:boundrhogen}, for every $n\in \N$, we have 
\begin{equation}\label{eq:nonoverfittingcondgen}
   A_T\;=\; \gamma \Delta_{MT}+\frac{1-\gamma}{8}\mu_{Q_TM}\;\geq\; \gamma\sum_{j=1}^b|M_j \setminus T_j|\kappa_j + \sum_{j=1}^b |T_j\setminus M_j |\left(\tfrac{1-\gamma}{8} n \rho(\bs X) \min_{i\in T_j\setminus M_j}{\beta^{\star}_{i}}^{2} - \gamma\kappa_j\right) 
\end{equation}
 Since $T \subseteq S^I \cup S^L$ and $M\cap S^I=T\cap S^I$, $T_j\setminus M_j \subseteq S^L_j$. By definition of $S^L_j$, the last term on the right in \eqref{eq:nonoverfittingcond} is nonnegative. Suppose $M\cap S^S\neq\emptyset$, by Lemma~\ref{lem:upperboundnoncentralgen}, we then have:
$$\frac{\mu_{Q_T T}}{2 A_T} \leq \frac{n\,\bar{\lambda}\,\sum_{j=1}^b|(S_j\cap M_j )\setminus T_j|\,\max_{i\in (S_j\cap M_j )\setminus T_j}{\beta_{i}^{\star}}^2}{2 \gamma\sum_{j=1}^b|M_j \setminus T_j|\kappa_j}$$
For all $j=1,\ldots,b$, $|M_j \setminus T_j| \geq |(S_j\cap M_j )\setminus T_j|$. Moreover, since $T\cap S^I=M\cap S^I$ and $S^L\subseteq T$, $(S_j\cap M_j )\setminus T_j \subseteq S^S_j$ for all $j=1,\ldots,b$. We get:
$$\frac{\mu_{Q_T T}}{2 A_T} \leq \frac{n\,\bar{\lambda}\,\sum_{j=1}^b|(S_j\cap M_j )\setminus T_j|\,\max_{i\in S^S_j}{\beta_{i}^{\star}}^2}{2 \gamma\sum_{j=1}^b|(S_j\cap M_j ) \setminus T_j|\kappa_j}$$
An upper bound on the right hand-side above follows from:
\begin{eqnarray*}
    \sum_{j=1}^b\frac{n\bar{\lambda}\max_{i\in S^S_j}{\beta_i^{\star}}^2}{2\gamma\kappa_j}
    &\geq &\sum_{j=1,|(S_j\cap M_j )\setminus T_j|\neq 0}^b\frac{|(S_j\cap M_j )\setminus T_j|n\bar{\lambda}\max_{i\in S^S_j}{\beta_i^{\star}}^2}{|(S_j\cap M_j )\setminus T_j|2\gamma\kappa_j} \\
    &=& \frac{\sum_{j=1,|(S_j\cap M_j )\setminus T_j|\neq 0 }^b|(S_j\cap M_j )\setminus T_j|n\bar{\lambda}\max_{i\in S^S_j}{\beta_i^{\star}}^2\Big(1+\sum_{l\neq j}\frac{|(S_l\cap M_l)\setminus T_l|2\gamma\kappa_l}{|(S_j\cap M_j )\setminus T_j|2\gamma\kappa_j}\Big)}{\sum_{j=1}^b|(S_j\cap M_j )\setminus T_j|2\gamma\kappa_j} \\
    &\geq & \frac{\sum_{j=1}^b|(S_j\cap M_j )\setminus T_j|n\bar{\lambda}\max_{i\in S^S_j}{\beta_i^{\star}}^2}{\sum_{j=1}^b \big|(S\cap M_j)\setminus T_j\big|2\gamma\kappa_j} 
\end{eqnarray*}
where last inequality follows from $\Big(1+\sum_{l\neq j}\frac{|(S_l\cap M_l)\setminus T_l|2\gamma\kappa_l}{|(S_j\cap M_j )\setminus T_j|2\gamma\kappa_j}\Big)\geq 1$ for all $j$ such that $|(S\cap M_j)\setminus T_j\big|\neq 0$. By definition of the $S_j^S$, $\sum_{j=1}^b\frac{n\bar{\lambda}\max_{i\in S^S_j}{\beta_i^{\star}}^2}{2\gamma\kappa_j}$ goes to 0 and so does $\frac{\mu_{Q_T T}}{2A_T}$. Then, when $M\cap S^S\neq\emptyset$, $\phi$ can be taken arbitrarily close to 1 for $n$ large enough. Observe that, when $M\setminus T\neq \emptyset$, by A6, for any $\phi\in(0,1)$, $\phi\gamma\sum_{j}|M_j\setminus T_j|\kappa_j\geq \max\{\tfrac{3}{4}|M\setminus T|,7\}$ as long as $\phi\min_j \ln(p_j-s_j)\geq 14$, which always holds in our asymptotic regime for $n$ sufficiently large. The last term in \eqref{eq:nonoverfittingcondgen} is also nonnegative and $\phi A_T\geq \max\{\tfrac{3}{4}|M\setminus S|,7\}$ for any $\phi\in(0,1)$ when $M\setminus T\neq \emptyset$. When $M\setminus T= \emptyset$, then $T\setminus M \neq \emptyset$ ($M\neq T$) and $\max\{\tfrac{3}{4}|M\setminus T|,7\}=7$. By definition of $S^L_j$, the last term in \eqref{eq:nonoverfittingcondgen} is larger than $7$ for $n$ sufficiently large. We also have $\phi A_T\geq \max\{\tfrac{3}{4}|M\setminus S|,7\}$ for any $\phi\in(0,1)$ and $n$ large enough when $M\setminus T = \emptyset$.

By Lemma~\ref{lem:boundexpnc2}, if $M\cap S^S=\emptyset$, for $n$ large enough
$$
\E(NC(M))\;\leq\;16 e^{-A_T}A_T^{|M\setminus T|/2+1}+e^{-A_T},
$$
If $M\cap S^S\neq\emptyset$, for any $\phi\in(0,1)$, by Lemma~\ref{lem:upperboundnoncentralgen} for $n$ large enough, $\mu_{Q_T,T}>1$ \paul{this is where $\beta_{min}$ bounded away from 0} and
$$\E\left(NC(M)\right)  \;\;\leq\;\;  2e^{-\phi A_T}[2(\phi+1)\,A_T]^{\frac{|M\setminus T|}{4}+1}+ e^{-A_T}$$


% We decompose $\mathcal{S}$ as follows:
% \begin{equation}\label{eq:decompS}
%     \mathcal{S} = \sum_{T\in \mathcal{T}}\mathcal{S}_T \quad\text{with}\quad \mathcal{S}_T=\sum_{M \not\in\mathcal{T} | M\cap S^I=T\cap S^I} 
% \end{equation}
\subsection{Proof of Theorem \ref{theo:empbayesselconsist}}

Denote 
\begin{equation}
    A^{\circ}_j = \sum_{M \in \mathcal{M} | |M_j| \geq s_j}\frac{|M_j|-s_j}{p_j}NC^{\circ}(M)\quad\text{and}\quad
    B^{\circ}_j = \sum_{M \in \mathcal{M} | |M_j| < s_j}\frac{s_j-|M_j|}{p_j}NC^{\circ}(M)
\end{equation}
From the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:shatconsistence} we have $ \frac{\widehat{s}_j}{p_j}  = \frac{s_j}{p_j}¡
+\,\, A^{\circ}_j-\,\,B^{\circ}_j  $ then:
\begin{align}\label{eq:decompstep2pen}
    \ln(\frac{p_j}{\hat{s_j}}-1) + \frac{1}{2}\ln(n) 
    &= \ln\Big(\frac{p_j-s_j}{s_j}\Big) + \frac{1}{2}\ln(n) + \ln\Big(\frac{p_j-\hat{s_j}}{p_j-s_j}\Big) + \ln\Big(\frac{s_j}{\hat{s_j}}\Big) \nonumber \\
    &= \ln(p_j-s_j)+\ln(\frac{\sqrt{n}}{s_j})+\ln(1-\frac{p_j}{p_j-s_j}(A^{\circ}_j-B^{\circ}_j))-\ln(1+\frac{p_j}{s_j}(A^{\circ}_j-B^{\circ}_j))
\end{align}
We start by showing the penalty in step 2 satisfies assumption~A6. To do so we first show that $\frac{p_j}{p_j-s_j}A^{\circ}_j\to c$ with $c <1$ with probability going to 1 for any $\kappa^{\circ}\to \infty$. From the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:shatconsistence}, $A^{\circ}_j\leq \frac{p_j-s_j}{p_j}$. Observe that $A^{\circ}_j \to \frac{p_j-s_j}{p_j}$ if and only if $\sum_{M\in \mathcal{M}||M_j|=p_j}NC(M)\to 1$ and $\sum_{M\in \mathcal{M}||M_j|<p_j}NC(M)\to 0$. Let $M'\in \mathcal{M}$ such that $|M'_j|=p_j$. Then $|M_j'\setminus S_j|= p_j-s_j$ and $p_j-s_j$ is larger than 1 for $n$ sufficiently large by assumption. Let $M''$ be any model $M \setminus\{i\}$ where $i\in |M_j'\setminus S_j|$. Since $|M_j''|<p_j$, we have for :
$$P(\frac{p_j}{p_j-s_j}A^{\circ}_j  \to 1) =P( \sum_{M\in \mathcal{M}||M_j|<p_j}NC(M) \to 0)\leq P(\frac{NC(M'')}{NC(M')} \to 0)$$

Observe that
$\frac{NC(M'')}{NC(M')}=e^{\kappa^{\circ}-\frac{1}{2}L_{M'M''}}$ and we have:
$$P(\frac{NC(M'')}{NC(M')} \to 0)\leq P(L_{M'M''}>2\kappa^{\circ})$$\paul{This is shady}
Since $M''\subset M'$, by Lemma~\ref{lemma:s7} $L_{M'M''}\sim \chi_{1}^2$. By assumption $\kappa^{\circ} >1$ for $n$ sufficiently large and by Lemma~\ref{lemma:s1}:
$$P(L_{M'M''}>2\kappa^{\circ}) \leq e^{\frac{1}{2}}{\kappa^{\circ}}^{\frac{1}{2}}e^{-\kappa^{\circ}}$$
Since by assumption $\kappa^{\circ} \to \infty$, $\frac{p_j}{p_j-s_j}A^{\circ}_j\to c$ with $c <1$ with probability going to 1. Morevover, from the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:shatconsistence}, $B^{\circ}_j \in [0,\frac{s_j}{p_j}]$ and, for every $j=1,\ldots,b$, from \eqref{eq:decompstep2pen}, an asymptotically well defined bound on the step 2 penalty is:
\begin{equation*}
  \ln(\frac{p_j}{\hat{s_j}}-1) + \frac{1}{2}\ln(n) \geq \ln(p_j-s_j)+\ln(\frac{\sqrt{n}}{s_j})+\ln(1-\frac{p_j}{p_j-s_j}A^{\circ}_j)-\ln(\frac{p_j}{s_j}A^{\circ}_j)  
\end{equation*}

% The problem with the empirical Bayes penalty lies in controlling the factor $\ln(\frac{\hat{s}}{s})$ where the "averaging" by $p$ is not present anymore.

% By \eqref{eq:boundsshat}, 
% $$\ln(\frac{p}{\hat{s}}-1) + \frac{1}{2}\ln(n) = \ln(p-s)+\ln(\frac{\sqrt{n}}{s})+\ln(1-\frac{1}{1-s/p}A^{\circ})-\ln(1+\frac{p}{s}A^{\circ})$$
% We now study the convergence of $  \E\big(p_jA^{\circ}_j\big)$ for every $j=1,\ldots,b$. We proceed as in the proof of
% Theorem~\ref{theo:suffcondlinearmodel} with the differences that pairwise comparisons of score values are now with respect to $S_j$ instead of $S$ and the set of models is restricted to models $M \in \mathcal{M}$ such that $|M_j| \geq s_j$.

% Observe that for any $M\in\mathcal{M}$:
% $$NC(M)=(1+\sum_{M' \neq M} e^{C(M')}/ e^{C(M)})^{-1} \leq (1+e^{C(S_j)-C(M)})^{-1}$$
% We have:
% $$C(S_j)-C(M)=\Delta_{MS}+\frac{1}{2}L_{S_jM}$$
% where $\Delta_{MS}=(|M_j|-s_j)\kappa^{\circ} + (|M|-|M_j|)\kappa^{\circ}$ and $L_{S_jM}=L_{S_jM_j}-L_{MM_j}$
% As in the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:boundexpnc}, we have:
% \begin{eqnarray*}
%   \E\left(NC(M)\right)  &\leq& \int_0^1 P\left((1+e^{C(S)-C(M)})^{-1}\geq u\right){\rm d} u\\
%   &=&\int_0^1 P\Big(-\tfrac12 L_{S_jM} \geq \Delta_{MS}-\ln{(\tfrac1u-1)}\Big) {\rm d}u.
% \end{eqnarray*}
% Let $Q_j=M_j \cup S_j$ then $L_{S_jM_j}=L_{Q_jM_j}-L_{Q_jS_j}$. For any $\gamma\in (0,1)$
% $$
% -\tfrac12 L_{S_jM} -(\Delta_{MS}-\ln{(\tfrac1u-1)})\;=\;\left(\tfrac12 L_{Q_jS_j}-\ln{\Bigl(\frac{e^{\gamma\Delta_{MS}}}{\tfrac1u-1}\Bigl)}\right)-\Bigl(\tfrac12 L_{Q_jM_j}+(1-\gamma)\Delta_{M}-\frac{1}{2}L{MM_j}\Bigl).
% $$
% % \begin{equation}
% %     L_{MS}-2\sum_{j=1}^b\Delta_{M,j}-2\ln{(1 / u-1)}= L_{Q_SM}-2\ln{\Bigl(\frac{e^{\gamma\sum_{j=1}^b\Delta_{M,j}}}{1 / u-1}\Bigl)}-\Bigl(L_{Q_SM}+2(1-\gamma)\sum_{j=1}^b\Delta_{M,j}\Bigl)
% % \end{equation}
% Observe that for any random variables $U$, $V$, and any $\epsilon,\gamma' \geq 0$, the event $
% \{U-V \geq 0\}$ implies $\{U \geq \gamma' \epsilon\}\cup
% \{V  < \gamma' \epsilon\}$. Let $U=\tfrac12 L_{Q_SS}-\ln{\Bigl(\frac{e^{\gamma\Delta_{MS}}}{\tfrac1u-1}\Bigl)}$ and $V=\tfrac12 L_{Q_SM}+(1-\gamma)\Delta_{M}$. Take $\epsilon=\mu_{Q_SM}$ and $\gamma'=\tfrac18({1-\gamma})$. Recall that $A=\gamma\Delta_{MS}+\gamma'\mu_{Q_SM}$ and
% $$
% \{U\geq \gamma'\epsilon\}\;=\;\{\tfrac12 L_{Q_SS}\geq \ln{\Bigl(\frac{e^{\gamma\Delta_{MS}}}{\tfrac1u-1}}\Bigl)+\gamma'\mu_{Q_SM}\}\;=\;\{\tfrac12 L_{Q_SS}\geq \ln{\Bigl(\frac{e^{A}}{\tfrac1u-1}}\Bigl)\},
% $$
% $$
% \{V<\gamma'\epsilon\}\;=\;\{\tfrac12 L_{Q_SM}<-(1-\gamma)\Delta_{MS}+\gamma'\mu_{Q_SM}\}\;=\;\{\tfrac12 L_{Q_SM}<\gamma'(\mu_{Q_SM}-8\Delta_{MS})\}.
% $$
% By the union bound we have:
% \begin{equation}\label{eq:prooflemmas20nonspu}
%     \E\left(NC(M)\right)  \leq \int_0^1P\Bigl(\tfrac12 L_{Q_SS}\geq \ln{\Bigl(\frac{e^{A}}{\tfrac1u-1}\Bigl)}\Bigl){\rm d}u+ P\Bigl(\tfrac12 L_{Q_SM}<\gamma'(\mu_{Q_SM}-8\Delta_{MS})\Bigl).
% \end{equation}
Since by assumption $\frac{\sqrt{n}}{s_j}\to \infty$, to show from the bound above that step 2 penalty satisfies assumption~A6, it is enough to show $\frac{p_j}{s_j}A^{\circ}_j$ is finite.

Splitting $A^{\circ}_j$ between models $M$ such that $|M|\leq s$ and $M$ such that $|M|> s$.
\begin{equation}\label{eq:decompAj}
    \E\big(\frac{p_j }{s_j} A^{\circ}_j\big) = \sum_{M \in \mathcal{M} | |M_j| \geq s_j, |M| \leq s}\frac{|M_j|-s_j}{s_j} \E(NC^{\circ}(M)) + \sum_{M \in \mathcal{M} | |M_j| \geq s_j, |M| > s}\frac{|M_j|-s_j}{s_j}\E(NC^{\circ}(M))
\end{equation}
A bound on the first term in the sum above is:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:boundfirstterm}
    \sum_{M \in \mathcal{M} | |M_j| \geq s_j, |M| \leq s}\frac{|M_j|-s_j}{s_j} \E(NC^{\circ}(M)) \leq \frac{s}{s_j}
\end{equation}
Since by assumption for all $j$, $\frac{s}{s_j}< \inf$, the first term in the right-hand side of \eqref{eq:decompAj} is finite.

Using that $\{M \in \mathcal{M} | |M_j| \geq s_j, |M| > s\} \subseteq \{M \in \mathcal{M} | |M| > s\}$, $|M_j|<|M|$ and multiplying and dividing by $s(k+1)$ for $k>2l^{-1}$, a bound on the second term in the right-hand side of \eqref{eq:decompAj} is:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:boundsecondterm}
  \sum_{M \in \mathcal{M} | |M_j| \geq s_j, |M| > s}\frac{|M_j|-s_j}{s_j}\E(NC^{\circ}(M))\leq \frac{s(k+1)}{s_j}\sum_{M \in \mathcal{M} ||M| > s}\frac{|M|-s_j}{s(k+1)}\E(NC^{\circ}(M) )  
\end{equation}

For any $M$ such that $|M|<s(k+1)$, $\frac{|M|-s_j}{s(k+1)} <1$, then:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:boundsecondterm2}
    \frac{s(k+1)}{s_j}\sum_{M \in \mathcal{M} ||M| > s}\frac{|M|-s_j}{s(k+1)}\E(NC^{\circ}(M) ) \leq \frac{s(k+1)}{s_j}\bigg(1+\sum_{M \in \mathcal{M} ||M| > s(k+1)}\frac{|M|-s_j}{s(k+1)}\E(NC^{\circ}(M) )\bigg)
\end{equation}

For any $M \in \mathcal{M}$ such that $|M_j| \geq s_j$ and $|M| > s$, denote:
$$D=\left(|M|-s\right)\kappa^{\circ}$$
Note that $D=\gamma\left(|M|-s\right)\kappa^{\circ} + \tfrac{1-\gamma}{8} \mu_{SM}$ for $\gamma=1$. Since $|M|> s$, $|M|-s$ is non-negative and by assumption $\kappa^{\circ}$ goes to infinity, so does $D$. Then for $n$ sufficiently large $D \geq \frac{3}{4}|M\setminus S|$, by Lemma~\ref{lem:boundexpnc}, then:
$$\E(N C(M)) \leq 16 e^{-D} D^{\frac{|M \setminus S|}{2}+1}+e^{-D}$$
We also have for $x$ sufficiently large and any $\alpha \in (0,1)$
\begin{equation}\label{eq:boundexp}
16e^{-x}x^{\tfrac12|M\setminus S|+1}+e^{-x}\leq e^{-\psi x}. 
\end{equation}
Since $k>2l^{-1}$, $\frac{k^{-1}+1}{l2^{-1}+1}<1$, take $\frac{k^{-1}+1}{l2^{-1}+1}< \psi <1 $:
$$\sum_{M \in \mathcal{M} ||M| > s(k+1)}\frac{|M|-s_j}{s(k+1)}\E(NC^{\circ}(M) ) \leq \sum_{M \in \mathcal{M} ||M| > s(k+1)}e^{-\psi \left(|M|-s\right)\kappa^{\circ}}$$
Note that:
$$\left(|M|-s\right)^{-1}\ln(\frac{|M|-s_j}{s(k+1)}) \leq \left(|M|-s\right)^{-1}\ln(\frac{|M|-s}{s(k+1)})$$
where the right hand-side is always smaller than $(s\,e(k+1))^{-1}$ and then:
$$\sum_{M \in \mathcal{M} ||M| > s(k+1)}\frac{|M|-s_j}{s(k+1)}\E(NC^{\circ}(M) ) \leq \sum_{M \in \mathcal{M} ||M| > s(k+1)}e^{-\left(|M|-s\right)\big(\psi \kappa^{\circ}-\left(s\,e(k+1)\right)^{-1})\big)}$$
We split the sum in the right hand-side into sums over the models that have the same size, that is the models $M$ such that $|M|-s= x$ for $x\in\{s\,k,\ldots,\min\{n,p\}\}$:
\begin{equation*}
    \sum_{M \in \mathcal{M} ||M| > s(k+1)}\frac{|M|-s_j}{s(k+1)}\E(NC^{\circ}(M) ) \leq \sum_{x=s(k+1)}^{\min\{n,p\}}\sum_{M \in \mathcal{M} ||M| =x }e^{-x\big(\psi\kappa^{\circ}-\left(s\,e(k+1)\right)^{-1})\big)}
\end{equation*}
The set of models $M \in \mathcal{M}$ such that $|M| =s+x$ has size at most $ {p \choose s+x}$ and $\ln{p \choose s+x}\leq (x+s) \ln(p(x+s)^{-1})$. We then get:
\begin{equation*}
    \sum_{M \in \mathcal{M} ||M| > s(k+1)}\frac{|M|-s_j}{s(k+1)}\E(NC^{\circ}(M) ) \leq \sum_{x=sk}^{\min\{n,p\}}e^{-x\big(\psi \kappa^{\circ}-(1+sx^{-1})\ln(p(x+s)^{-1})-\left(s\,e(k+1)\right)^{-1})\big)}
\end{equation*}
Since $x\geq s\,k$,
\begin{equation*}
\sum_{M \in \mathcal{M} ||M| > s(k+1)}\frac{|M|-
s_j}{s(k+1)}\E(NC^{\circ}(M) ) \leq \sum_{x=sk}^{\min\{n,p\}}e^{-x\big(\psi \kappa^{\circ}-(1+k^{-1})\ln\big(\frac{p}{k(s+1)}\big)-\left(s\,e(k+1)\right)^{-1})\big)}
\end{equation*}
By assumption $s\geq1$ for $n$ sufficiently large and $\kappa^{\circ}=\ln(p\sqrt{n})$, then:
$$\psi \kappa^{\circ}-(1+k^{-1})\ln\big(\frac{p}{k(s+1)} \big)\geq \psi \kappa^{\circ}-(1+k^{-1})\ln\big(\frac{p}{k}\big) =   \ln\bigg[k^{1+\frac{1}{k}}\frac{n^{\psi/2}}{p^{1-\psi+\frac{1}{k}}}\bigg]$$
Moreover:
$$\frac{n^{\psi/2}}{p^{1-\psi+\frac{1}{k}}}=\frac{n}{p^{2(\psi^{-1}-1+\frac{1}{k\psi})}}$$
Since $\psi> \frac{k^{-1}+1}{l2^{-1}+1}$,  $l > 2(\frac{1}{\psi}-1+\frac{1}{\psi k})$ and
$$\psi \kappa^{\circ}-(1+k^{-1})\ln\big(\frac{p}{k(s+1)} \big)\geq \ln\bigg[k^{1+\frac{1}{k}}\frac{n}{p^l}\bigg]$$
we have for any $x\leq 1$ and for $n$ sufficiently large:
$$e^{-x\big(\psi \kappa^{\circ}-(1+k^{-1})\ln\big(\frac{p}{k(s+1)}\big)-\left(s\,e(k+1)\right)^{-1})\big)}\leq e^{-x\big(\frac{n}{p^l}-\left(s\,e(k+1)\right)^{-1})\big)}\leq 1$$
where the last inequality follows from assumptions $\frac{n}{p^l}\to\infty$ and $s\leq 1$. By the properties of geometric series,
$$\sum_{M \in \mathcal{M} ||M| > s(k+1)}\frac{|M|-
s_j}{s(k+1)}\E(NC^{\circ}(M) ) \leq \frac{1-e^{-\min\{n,p\}\big(\frac{n}{p^l}-\left(s\,e(k+1)\right)^{-1})\big)}}{1-e^{-\big(\frac{n}{p^l}-\left(s\,e(k+1)\right)^{-1})\big)}}$$
where the right hand-side above is smaller than 2 for $n$ sufficiently large.
From bounds \eqref{eq:boundsecondterm} and \eqref{eq:boundsecondterm2}, for $n$ sufficiently large and any $k>2l^{-1}$, we then get: 
\begin{equation}\label{eq:finalboundsecondterm}
     \sum_{M \in \mathcal{M} | |M_j| \geq s_j, |M| > s}\frac{|M_j|-s_j}{s_j}\E(NC^{\circ}(M)) \leq \frac{3s(k+1)}{s_j}
\end{equation}
By equation \eqref{eq:decompAj} and bounds \eqref{eq:boundfirstterm} and \eqref{eq:finalboundsecondterm}, for $n$ sufficiently large and any $k>2l^{-1}$, we have:
$$\E(\frac{p_j}{s_j}A_j)\leq (3k+4)\frac{s}{s_j}$$
By assumption for any $j=1,\ldots,b$, $\frac{s}{s_j}<+\infty$, then $\E(\frac{p_j}{s_j}A_j) < \infty$ and $\frac{p_j}{s_j}A^{\circ}_j$ is almost surely finite. 

We go on with showing the $\beta_{\min,j}$ satisfy condition $A7$. Since $A_j^{\circ} \geq 0$, from \eqref{eq:decompstep2pen}, for every $j=1,\ldots,b$:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:lowerboundstep2pen}
    \ln(\frac{p_j}{\hat{s_j}}-1) + \frac{1}{2}\ln(n) \leq \ln\big(\frac{p_j-s_j}{s_j}\big)+\frac{1}{2}\ln(n)+\ln\bigg(\frac{1+\frac{p_j}{p_j-s_j}B^{\circ}_j}{1-\frac{p_j}{s_j}B^{\circ}_j}\bigg)
\end{equation}
where $\frac{p_j}{s_j}B^{\circ}_j=\pi_j$ and  $\frac{p_j}{p_j-s_j}B^{\circ}_j=\frac{s_j}{p_j-s_j}\pi_j$. From \eqref{eq:lowerboundstep2pen} and than $p>\frac{p_j-s_j}{s_j}$ for every $j=1,\ldots,b$, we have:
\begin{equation*}
    \frac{1}{8}\min_j \frac{1}{2}\frac{\ln(\sqrt{n})}{\ln(\frac{p_j}{\widehat{s}_j})+\ln(\sqrt{n})}
\geq \frac{1}{16}\frac{\ln(\sqrt{n})}{\ln(p)+\ln(\sqrt{n})+\max_j\ln\bigg(\frac{1+\frac{s_j}{p_j-s_j}\pi_j}{1-
\pi_j}\bigg)}
\end{equation*}
By A8, for $n$ large enough $n\geq p^l$ and $\frac{\ln(p)}{\ln(\sqrt{n})}\leq \frac{2}{l}$ and then:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:boundlog}
    \frac{1}{8}\min_j \frac{1}{2}\frac{\ln(\sqrt{n})}{\ln(p_j-s_j)+\ln(\sqrt{n})}
\geq \frac{1}{16}\bigg[1+2l^{-1}+2\ln^{-1}(n)\max_j\ln\bigg(\frac{1+\frac{s_j}{p_j-s_j}\pi_j}{1-\pi_j}\bigg)\bigg]^{-1}
\end{equation}

By \eqref{eq:boundlog} and \eqref{eq:lowerboundstep2pen} assumption~A9 is sufficient for assumption~A7 to hold.
