\section{Proofs of auxiliary results}\label{sec:proofauxi}

\subsection{Proof of Lemma~\ref{lemma:3tailbounds}}

	a) \,\, By union bound and the identical distribution of the $y_i$'s,
\begin{center}
    $P\left(\underset{i\in\{1,\ldots,p\}}{\max}|y_i|> a \right)     \leq \sum_{i=1}^{p}{ P\left(|\sqrt{n}y_i|>\sqrt{n}a \right)}
    = p\; P\left(|z|>\sqrt{n}a\right)$
\end{center}
    where $z\sim N(0,1)$. 
    By symmetry and the standard tail bound for standard normal, $P(z \geq \delta) \leq \frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}} \frac{1}{\delta} e^{-\delta^{2} / 2}$:
\begin{center}
  $P\left(\underset{i\in\{1,\ldots,p\}}{\max}|y_i|> a \right) 
     \leq  p\; 2 \, P\left(z>\sqrt{n}a\right) \leq \frac{1}{\sqrt{\pi}}\frac{\sqrt{2}}{\sqrt{n}a}p e^{-\frac{n}{2} a^{2}}$
\end{center}
Since $a \geq \sqrt{\frac{2\ln(p)}{n}}$, $\frac{\sqrt{2}}{\sqrt{n}a}\leq \frac{1}{\ln(p)}$. Taking $a^2=a^2-\frac{2\ln(p)}{n}+\frac{2\ln(p)}{n}$, we get \begin{center}
$ P\left(\underset{i\in\{1,\ldots,p\}}{\max}|y_i|> a \right) \leq \frac{1}{\sqrt{\pi\ln(p)}} e^{-\frac{n}{2} \left(a^{2}-\frac{2\ln(p)}{n}\right)}$ \\\end{center}
b) $ \underset{i \in \{1,\ldots,s\}}{\min}|\beta_i|- \underset{i \in \{1,\ldots,s\}}{\max}|y_i-\beta_i| > a$ implies $\forall i \; \underset{i \in \{1,\ldots,s\}}{\min}|\beta_i| - |y_i-\beta_i| > a $. Since $\forall i \; |\beta_i|< |\beta_i-y_i|+|y_i|$ we have that $ \forall i \; \min_{i \in \{1,\ldots,s\}}|\beta_i| - |y_i-\beta_i| > a$ implies $\forall i \; |y_i| > a $ and $ \underset{i \in \{1,\ldots,s\}}{\min}|y_i| > a$. By the properties of the probability $P$: 
\begin{center}$ P\left(\underset{i \in \{1,\ldots,s\}}{\min}|\beta_i|-\underset{i \in \{1,\ldots,s\}}{\max}|y_i-\beta_i| > a\right) \leq P\left(\underset{i \in \{1,\ldots,s\}}{\min}|y_i| > a\right) $ \end{center}
c)\,\, We start by bounding the probability $P(|y_i|)>a$ for any $i$. We have that:
$$P(|y_i|>a) = P(y_i>a) + P(y_i<-a) = P(z>\frac{a-\mu}{\sigma}) + P(z<-\frac{a+\mu}{\sigma}) \quad \text{where }z\sim N(0,1)$$
If $\mu>0$, then $P(|y_i|>a) =  P(z>\frac{a-|\mu|}{\sigma}) + P(z<-\frac{a+|\mu|}{\sigma})$. If $\mu<0$, by symmetry, $P(z>\frac{a-\mu}{\sigma})=P(z>\frac{a+|\mu|}{\sigma})=P(z<-\frac{a+|\mu|}{\sigma})$ and $P(z<-\frac{a+\mu}{\sigma})=P(z<\frac{|\mu|-a}{\sigma})=P(z>\frac{a-|\mu|}{\sigma})$. Then for any $\mu$, $P(|y_i|>a)  = P(z>\frac{a-|\mu|}{\sigma}) + P(z<-\frac{a+|\mu|}{\sigma}) $ and using the symmetry of the standard Gaussian for any $a<|\mu|$: 
\begin{equation}\label{eq:random}
    P(|y_i|>a) = 1+P(0<z<\frac{|\mu|-a}{\sigma})-P(0<z<\frac{a+|\mu|}{\sigma})
\end{equation}
From \cite{chu}, for any $\delta>0$, $\frac{1}{2}\left(1-e^{-\delta^2/2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq P(z \leq \delta)-\frac{1}{2}\leq \frac{1}{2}\left(1-e^{-2\delta^2/\pi}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}$. Thus, for any $\gamma,\delta>0$:
$$ P(0<z \leq \gamma)-P(0<z \leq \delta)\leq
\frac{1}{2}\left(1-e^{-2\gamma^2/\pi}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}-\frac{1}{2}\left(1-e^{-\delta^2/2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}$$
Applying the above to \eqref{eq:random}, we get:
$$P(|y_i|>a) \leq 
1+ \frac{1}{2}\left(\left(1-e^{-2(|\mu|-a)^2/\pi\sigma^2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}-\left(1-e^{-(a+|\mu|)^2/2\sigma^2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)\\$$

Using that $x^{\frac{1}{2}}-y^{\frac{1}{2}}=\frac{x-y}{x^{\frac{1}{2}}+y^{\frac{1}{2}}}$ for $x,y>0$, we get:
$$P(|y_i|>a) \leq 1- \frac{e^{-2(|\mu|-a)^2/\pi\sigma^2}-e^{-(a+|\mu|)^2/2\sigma^2}}{2\left(\left(1-e^{-2(|\mu|-a)^2/\pi\sigma^2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\left(1-e^{-(a+|\mu|)^2/2\sigma^2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)}$$

Finally, $P(\min_{i}|y_i|>a)=\exp\left(s\ln{P(|y_i|>a)}\right)$, using that $\ln\left(1+ x \right)< x$ for $x>-1$ we get the desired inequality.

\subsection{Proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:L0toL1convergencegen}}
Suppose $NC(M_1)+\ldots+NC(M_k)>\frac{k}{k+1}$ then for any $M\neq \{M_1,\ldots,M_k\}$, $NC(M)<\frac{1}{k+1}$. In addition, if $NC(M_1)+\ldots+NC(M_k)>\frac{k}{k+1}$ then necessarily $\max_{l=1,\ldots,k}NC(M_l)> \frac{1}{k+1}$ and then $\hat{S}^{b}\in \{M_1,\ldots,M_k\}$. Consequently, 
$$
P(\hat{S}^b\not\in \{M_1,\ldots,M_k\})\; \leq\; P\Big(NC(M_1)+\ldots+NC(M_k)\leq\frac{k}{k+1}\Big)$$
We have:
$$P\Big(NC(M_1)+\ldots+NC(M_k)\leq\frac{k}{k+1}\Big)\;=\;P\Big(\sum_{M \in \mathcal{M}\setminus \{M_1,\ldots,M_k\}} NC(M)\geq \frac{1}{k+1}\Big)$$
The result follows from the the Markov's inequality applied to the right-hand side above.

\subsection{Proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:boundrhogen}} 
Clearly $M \subseteq Q_T$ then $\mu_{Q_TM}$, as defined in \eqref{eq:muQM}, satisfies
\begin{eqnarray*}
    \mu_{Q_TM}&=&\|\big(I_n-P_M\big) \bs X_{Q_T\setminus M} \bs \beta^{\star}_{Q_T\setminus M}\|^2\\
    &=&\|\big(I_n-P_M\big) \bs X_{T\setminus M} \bs \beta^{\star}_{T\setminus M}\|^2\\
    &= & n {\beta^{\star}_{T\setminus M}}^\top \left(\tfrac1n \bs X_{T\setminus M}^\top(I_n-P_M)\bs X_{T\setminus M}\right)\beta^{\star}_{T\setminus M}\\
    &\geq & n\lambda_{\min}\left(\tfrac1n \bs X_{T\setminus M}^\top(I_n-P_M)\bs X_{T\setminus M}\right)\|\beta^{\star}_{T\setminus M}\|^2,
\end{eqnarray*}
where the second equality follows from observing that $Q_T\setminus M=T\setminus M$. Since $T$ is a susbet of $S$, by reordering columns, $X_{S\setminus M}=[X_{T\setminus M},X_{S\setminus (M\cup T)}]$ and $\tfrac1n \bs X_{T\setminus M}^\top(I_n-P_M)\bs X_{T\setminus M}$ is a diagonal block of $\tfrac1n \bs X_{S\setminus M}^\top(I_n-P_M)\bs X_{S\setminus M}$. By interlacing, $\lambda_{\min}\left(\tfrac1n \bs X_{T\setminus M}^\top(I_n-P_M)\bs X_{T\setminus M}\right) \geq \lambda_{\min}\left(\tfrac1n \bs X_{S\setminus M}^\top(I_n-P_M)\bs X_{S\setminus M}\right)$. Bounding 
$\lambda_{\min}\left(\tfrac1n \bs X_{T\setminus M}^\top(I_n-P_M)\bs X_{T\setminus M}\right) \geq \rho(\bs X)$ and $\|\beta^{\star}_{T\setminus M}\|^2\geq \sum_{j=1}^b|T_j\setminus M_j|\,\min_{i\in T_j\setminus M_j}{\beta_{i}^{\star}}^2$, gives the desired result.

\subsection{Proof of Lemma~\ref{lemma:s1s3}}

See Lemma S1 for the case $\mu=0$ and Lemma S3 for the case $\mu>0$ in \cite{rossell2022concentration}.

\subsection{Proofs of Lemma~\ref{lemma:s2}}
See Lemma S2 in \cite{rossell2022concentration}.



\subsection{Proof of Lemma~\ref{lemma:s18}}
 When $\mu=0$, for any $u>\underline{u}\geq(1+ge^{-\nu/2})^{-1}$, $w(u)>\nu$. By Lemma~\ref{lemma:s1s3}, for any $u\in(\underline{u},\bar{u})$, $W$ satisfies the conditions of Lemma S18 in \cite{rossell2022concentration} with $d=2$, $l=1/2$, $c=\nu/2$ and $b=(e/\nu)^{\nu/2}$. The results follows from Lemma S18 for the case $ld=1$.\\

When $\mu>0$, let $w(u)=2\ln\big(g(u^{-1}-1)^{-1}\big)$. For $u\geq\underline{u}\geq(1+g^\gamma e^{-\nu/2})^{-1}$, $w(u)\geq 2(1-\gamma)\ln(g)+\nu>\mu+\nu$ and by Lemma~\ref{lemma:s1s3}: $P(W>w(u)) \leq be^{-l(u) w(u)}w(u)^c$ with  $l(u)=\big(1-\sqrt{\frac{\mu}{w(u)}}\big)^2/2$, $c=\nu/4$ and $b=\mu^{-\nu/4}$. Since $w(u)$ and $l(u)$ are increasing in $u$:
\begin{equation}
    \int_{\underline{u}}^{\bar{u}} P\left(W>2\ln \left(\frac{g}{1 / u-1}\right)\right) d u<\left[w(\bar{u})\right]^c \frac{b}{g^{2l(\underline{u})}} \int_{\underline{u}}^{\bar{u}}(1 / u-1)^{2l(\bar{u})} d u .
\end{equation}
Replacing $w(\bar{u})$ by its value we get:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:random}
    \int_{\underline{u}}^{\bar{u}} P\left(W>2\ln \left(\frac{g}{1 / u-1}\right)\right) d u<\left[2\ln \left(\frac{g}{1 / \bar{u}-1}\right)\right]^c \frac{b}{g^{2l(\underline{u})}} \int_{\underline{u}}^{\bar{u}}(1 / u-1)^{l(\bar{u}) d} d u .
\end{equation}

Applying the change of variables $v=1 / u-1$ gives
$$
\int_{\underline{u}}^{\bar{u}}(1 / u-1)^{l d} d u=\int_{1 / \bar{u}-1}^{1 / \underline{u}-1} \frac{v^{l(\bar{u}) d}}{(v+1)^2} d v
%<\int_{1 / \bar{u}-1}^{1 / \underline{u}-1} v^{l(\bar{u}) d-2} d v
$$
Note that $v^{l(\bar{u}) d}=(v-1 + 1)^{l(\bar{u}) d}$ with, $v-1>-1$ for $v<1 / \bar{u}-1$ since $\bar{u}<1$. We also have $l(\bar{u})d<1$ since $l(\bar{u})<1/2$. Then $v^{l(\bar{u}) d}=(v-1 + 1)^{l(\bar{u}) d}\leq 1 + l(\bar{u})d(v-1)\leq 1 + l(\bar{u})d(v+1)$. As a result:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:random2}
\int_{\underline{u}}^{\bar{u}}(1 / u-1)^{l 2} d u<\int_{1 / \bar{u}-1}^{1 / \underline{u}-1} \frac{1}{(v+1)^2} +\frac{l(\bar{u})d}{v+1} d v = \bar{u}-\underline{u}+l(\bar{u})d\ln\Big(\frac{\bar{u}}{\underline{u}}\Big)
%<\int_{1 / \bar{u}-1}^{1 / \underline{u}-1} v^{l(\bar{u}) d-2} d v
\end{equation}

% Finally, 
% \begin{equation}\label{eq:random2}
% \int_{1 / \bar{u}-1}^{1 / \underline{u}-1} v^{l(\bar{u}) d-2} d v=\frac{1}{1-l(\bar{u}) d}\left[\left(\frac{1}{\bar{u}}-1\right)^{l(\bar{u}) d-1}-\left(\frac{1}{\underline{u}}-1\right)^{l(\bar{u}) d-1}\right]
% \end{equation}
The results follows inputing the bound from \eqref{eq:random2} in \eqref{eq:random} and replacing $b$ by its value.


-\subsection{Proof of Lemma~\ref{lemma:s20}}
Since a probability is bounded by $1$, for any $a\in(0,1)$.
\begin{equation}\label{eq:basebound}
    \int_0^1 P\left(W>2 \ln \left(\frac{g}{1 / u-1}\right)\right) d u \;\leq\; 2 a+\int_{a}^{1-a} P\left(W>2\ln\left(\frac{g}{1 / u-1}\right)\right) d u.
\end{equation}

We first consider the case $\mu=0$. Take  $a=\left(1+g e^{-\nu / 2}\right)^{-1}$ where since $\ln(g)\geq 3\nu/4$, $a<1/2$. By Lemma~\ref{lemma:s18} with $\underline{u}=a$ and $\bar{u}=1-a$:
\begin{equation*} 
\int_0^1 P\left(W>2 \ln \left(\frac{g}{1 / u-1}\right)\right) d u \;<\; \frac{2}{1+g e^{-\nu / 2}}+\frac{1}{g} \ln \left(1+g e^{-\nu / 2}\right)\left[\frac{4 e}{\nu} \ln \left(ge^{-\nu / 4}\right)\right]^{\frac{\nu}{2}}.
\end{equation*}
Note that $\ln \left(1+g e^{-\nu / 2}\right) = \ln \left(g e^{-\nu / 4}\right)+\ln \left(\frac{1}{g e^{-\nu / 4}}+ e^{-\nu / 4}\right)$. Since $\ln(g) \geq 3\nu/4$, $\ln(g) > \nu/4-\ln(1-e^{-\nu/4})$, then $\frac{1}{g e^{-\nu / 4}}+ e^{-\nu / 4}<1$ and $\ln \left(1+g e^{-\nu / 2}\right)<\ln \left(g e^{-\nu / 4}\right)$. Then:

\begin{equation*}
\int_0^1 P\left(W>2 \ln \left(\frac{g}{1 / u-1}\right)\right) d u < 
\frac{2}{g e^{-\nu / 2}}+\frac{1}{g} \left(\frac{4 e}{\nu}\right)^{\frac{\nu}{2}} \ln^{\frac{\nu}{2}+1}\left(ge^{-\nu / 4}\right)
\end{equation*}

Since $\nu \geq 1$ and $\ln(g) \geq 3\nu/4$ then $\ln(g) \geq 2^{\frac{2}{2+\nu}} \left(\frac{\nu}{4}\right)^{\frac{\nu}{\nu+2}}+\nu/4$ and $\frac{2}{g e^{-\nu / 2}}\leq \frac{1}{g} \left(\frac{4 e}{\nu}\right)^{\frac{\nu}{2}} \ln^{\frac{\nu}{2}+1}\left(ge^{-\nu / 4}\right)$ and 
\begin{equation*}
\int_0^1 P\left(W>2 \ln \left(\frac{g}{1 / u-1}\right)\right) d u < \frac{2}{g} \left(\frac{4 e}{\nu}\right)^{\frac{\nu}{2}} \ln^{\frac{\nu}{2}+1}\left(ge^{-\nu / 4}\right)
\end{equation*}
Simple analysis of $\left(\frac{4 e}{\nu}\right)^{\frac{\nu}{2}}$ as a function of $\nu$ shows it reaches it maximum for $\nu=4$ and thus $\left(\frac{4 e}{\nu}\right)^{\frac{\nu}{2}}\leq e^2 < 8$. We also have $e^{-\nu / 4} <1$, then:
\begin{equation*}
\int_0^1 P\left(W>2 \ln \left(\frac{g}{1 / u-1}\right)\right) d u < \frac{16}{g} [\ln\left(g\right)]^{\frac{\nu}{2}+1}
\end{equation*}

We now consider the case $\mu>0$. In \eqref{eq:basebound}, take  $a=\left(1+g^{\phi}e^{-\nu/ 2}\right)^{-1}$ for some $\phi\in(0,1)$ where since $\phi\ln(g)> \nu/2$, $a\in(0,1/2)$. In Lemma~\ref{lemma:s18}, take $\underline{u}=a$ and $\bar{u}=1-a$. We have  $2 \ln \left(\frac{g}{1 / \bar{u}-1}\right)=2\ln(g^{\phi+1}e^{-\nu/2})$ and $l(\bar{u})2=\big(1-\sqrt{\frac{\mu}{2(1-\phi)\ln(g)+\nu}}\big)^2<1$ and $\ln\big(\frac{\bar{u}}{\underline{u}}\big)=\phi\ln(g)-\tfrac{1}{2}\nu$. We get:
$$
\int_0^1 P\left(W>2 \ln \left(\frac{g}{1 / u-1}\right)\right) d u \;\leq\; \frac{2}{1+g^{\phi}e^{-\nu/2}}+ 
\frac{1-2\underline{u}+\phi\ln(g)-\nu/2}{\mu^{\nu/4}g^{(1-\sqrt{\frac{\mu}{2(1-\phi)\ln(g)+\nu}})^2}} \left[2\ln\big(g^{(\phi+1)}e^{-\nu/2}\big)\right]^{\frac{\nu}{4}} $$

Since $\phi\ln(g)> \nu/2$, $1-2\underline{u}\leq \phi\ln(g)-\nu/2 \leq \ln(g^{\phi+1}e^{\nu/2})$ and $1+g^{\phi}e^{-\nu/2}>g^{\phi}e^{-\nu/2}$ we have:
$$
\int_0^1 P\left(W>2 \ln \left(\frac{g}{1 / u-1}\right)\right) d u \;\leq\; \frac{2}{g^{\phi}e^{-\nu/2}}+ 
\frac{\left[2\ln\big(g^{(\phi+1)}e^{-\nu/2}\big)\right]^{\frac{\nu}{4}+1}}{\mu^{\nu/4}g^{(1-\sqrt{\frac{\mu}{2(1-\phi)\ln(g)+\nu}})^2}}  $$
Observe that since $\phi < \Big(1-\big(\frac{\mu}{2\ln(g)}\big)^{\frac{1}{3}}\Big)^2$, $(1-\sqrt{\phi})^{-2}<\mu^{-1}(2(1-\phi)\ln(g))<\mu^{-1}(2(1-\phi)\ln(g))+\nu$ and then $\phi<\big(1-\sqrt{\frac{\mu}{2(1-\phi)\ln(g)+\nu}}\big)^2$. Using the later and $\mu \geq1$ and $e^{-\nu/2}\leq 1$:
$$
\int_0^1 P\left(W>2 \ln \left(\frac{g}{1 / u-1}\right)\right) d u \;\leq\; g^{-\phi}\left(2e^{\nu/2}+ 
\left[2\ln\big(g^{(\phi+1)}\big)\right]^{\frac{\nu}{4}+1}  \right)$$
Finally, since $\nu \geq 1$, $7>2^{-\frac{\nu}{\nu+4}}e^{\frac{2\nu}{\nu+4}}$ and $(\phi+1)\ln(g)>\phi\ln(g)\geq 7$, $\left[2\ln\big(g^{(\phi+1)}\big)\right]^{\frac{\nu}{4}+1}>2e^{\nu/2}$ and we get:
$$
\int_0^1 P\left(W>2 \ln \left(\frac{g}{1 / u-1}\right)\right) d u \;\leq\; 2g^{-\phi} 
\left[2\ln\big(g^{(\phi+1)}\big)\right]^{\frac{\nu}{4}+1}  $$



\subsection{Proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:boundexpnc}}
% ----------------
%  Although $\mu_{Q_SM}$ was defined for $M\subseteq Q_S$, in what follows we use the same notation for arbitrary two subsets. 
%  Note that $\mu_{Q_SM}=0$ automatically when $Q_S\setminus M\subseteq V\setminus S$ simply because $\beta^\star_{V\setminus S}=0$.
%  -----------------------------


By the second part of Lemma~\ref{lem:L0toL1convergence},  $NC(M)\leq (1+e^{C(S)-C(M)})^{-1}$ for any $M\in \mathcal M\setminus \{S\}$.    By the standard formula for expectation of a nonnegative random variable in terms of its CDF, we get:
\begin{eqnarray*}
  \E\left(NC(M)\right)  &\leq& \int_0^1 P\left((1+e^{C(S)-C(M)})^{-1}\geq u\right){\rm d} u\\
  &=&\int_0^1 P\left(C(S)-C(M) \leq \ln{(\tfrac1u-1)}\right){\rm d}u\\
  &=&\int_0^1 P\Big(-\tfrac12 L_{SM} \geq \Delta_{MS}-\ln{(\tfrac1u-1)}\Big) {\rm d}u.
\end{eqnarray*}
Let $Q_S=M \cup S$ and recall that $L_{SM}=L_{Q_SM}-L_{Q_SS}$. For any $\gamma\in (\frac{1}{2},1]$
$$
-\tfrac12 L_{SM} -(\Delta_{MS}-\ln{(\tfrac1u-1)})\;=\;\left(\tfrac12 L_{Q_SS}-\ln{\Bigl(\frac{e^{\gamma\Delta_{MS}}}{\tfrac1u-1}\Bigl)}\right)-\Bigl(\tfrac12 L_{Q_SM}+(1-\gamma)\Delta_{MS}\Bigl).
$$
% \begin{equation}
%     L_{MS}-2\sum_{j=1}^b\Delta_{M,j}-2\ln{(1 / u-1)}= L_{Q_SM}-2\ln{\Bigl(\frac{e^{\gamma\sum_{j=1}^b\Delta_{M,j}}}{1 / u-1}\Bigl)}-\Bigl(L_{Q_SM}+2(1-\gamma)\sum_{j=1}^b\Delta_{M,j}\Bigl)
% \end{equation}
Observe that for any random variables $U$, $V$, and any $\epsilon,\gamma' \geq 0$, the event $
\{U-V \geq 0\}$ implies $\{U \geq \gamma' \epsilon\}\cup
\{V  < \gamma' \epsilon\}$. Let $U=\tfrac12 L_{Q_SS}-\ln{\Bigl(\frac{e^{\gamma\Delta_{MS}}}{\tfrac1u-1}\Bigl)}$ and $V=\tfrac12 L_{Q_SM}+(1-\gamma)\Delta_{MS}$. Take $\epsilon=\mu_{Q_SM}$ and $\gamma'=\tfrac18({1-\gamma})$. Recall that $A_S=\gamma\Delta_{MS}+\gamma'\mu_{Q_SM}$ and
$$
\{U\geq \gamma'\epsilon\}\;=\;\{\tfrac12 L_{Q_SS}\geq \ln{\Bigl(\frac{e^{\gamma\Delta_{MS}}}{\tfrac1u-1}}\Bigl)+\gamma'\mu_{Q_SM}\}\;=\;\{\tfrac12 L_{Q_SS}\geq \ln{\Bigl(\frac{e^{A_S}}{\tfrac1u-1}}\Bigl)\},
$$
$$
\{V<\gamma'\epsilon\}\;=\;\{\tfrac12 L_{Q_SM}<-(1-\gamma)\Delta_{MS}+\gamma'\mu_{Q_SM}\}\;=\;\{\tfrac12 L_{Q_SM}<\gamma'(\mu_{Q_SM}-8\Delta_{MS})\}.
$$
By the union bound we have:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:prooflemmas20nonspu}
    \E\left(NC(M)\right)  \leq \int_0^1P\Bigl(\tfrac12 L_{Q_SS}\geq \ln{\Bigl(\frac{e^{A_S}}{\tfrac1u-1}\Bigl)}\Bigl){\rm d}u+ P\Bigl(\tfrac12 L_{Q_SM}<\gamma'(\mu_{Q_SM}-8\Delta_{MS})\Bigl).
\end{equation}
We first bound the first term in \eqref{eq:prooflemmas20nonspu}. If $M \subset S$ then $Q_S=S$, $L_{Q_SS}=0$, and this term is zero. Suppose now that $S$ is a strict subset of $Q_S$. By Lemma~\ref{lemma:s7}, $L_{Q_SS}$ has a  $\chi^2$-distribution. More precisely, $L_{Q_SS} \sim \mathcal{\chi}_{|Q_S\setminus S|}^2(\mu_{Q_SS})$ {but $\mu_{Q_SS}=0$ because $\beta^\star_{Q_S\setminus S}=0$}. Let $\nu=|M\setminus S|$. By assumption we have $A_S \geq \tfrac34|M\setminus S|=\tfrac34\nu$. From Lemma~\ref{lemma:s20},
\begin{equation}\label{eq:boundLQS}
\int_0^1P\Bigl(L_{Q_SS}>2\ln{\Bigl(\frac{e^{A_S}}{1 / u-1}\Bigl)}\Bigl){\rm d}u \;<\; 16
e^{-A_S}A_S^{\frac{|M\setminus S|}{2}+1}.
\end{equation}
% or, equivalently, 
% \begin{equation}\label{eq:boundLQS}
% \int_0^1P\Bigl(L_{Q_SS}>2\ln{\Bigl(\frac{e^{\gamma\Delta_{M}+\gamma'\mu_{Q_SM}}}{1 / u-1}\Bigl)}\Bigl){\rm d}u \;<\; 16
% e^{-\gamma\Delta_{M}+\gamma'\mu_{Q_SM}}\left(\gamma\Delta_{M}+\gamma'\mu_{Q_SM}\right)^{\frac{|M\setminus S|}{2}+1}.
% \end{equation}
We now bound the second term in \eqref{eq:prooflemmas20nonspu}. If $S \subset M$, then $Q_S=M$, $L_{Q_SM}=0$, $\mu_{Q_SM}=0$, and this term is zero. If $M$ is a strict subset of $Q_S$ then, by Lemma~\ref{lemma:s7}, $L_{Q_SM} \sim \chi_{|Q_S\setminus M|}^2(\mu_{Q_SM})$ %(note that $Q_S\setminus M=S\setminus M$ and $\mu_{Q_SM}=\mu_{Q_SM}$). 
Clearly, when $\mu_{Q_SM}\leq 8\Delta_{MS}$ or $\gamma = 1$, this term is also zero so suppose  $\mu_{Q_SM}> 8\Delta_{MS}$ and $\gamma <1$.  
We have  
$$P\left(L_{Q_SM}<2\gamma'(\mu_{Q_SM}-8\Delta_{MS})\right)\; \leq \; P\left(L_{Q_SM}<2\gamma'\mu_{Q_SM}\right)$$ 
and, by Lemma~\ref{lemma:s2}, as $\gamma'\in (0,\tfrac{1}{16})$
$$P\left(L_{Q_SM}<2\gamma'\mu_{Q_SM}\right) \;\;\leq\;\; (2\gamma')^{\frac{|S\setminus M|}{4}} e^{-\frac{1}{2}(1-\sqrt{2\gamma'})^2\mu_{Q_SM}}\;\;\leq\;\;  (\tfrac12)^{\tfrac34|S\setminus M|}e^{-\frac{1}{2}(1-\sqrt{2\gamma'})^2\mu_{Q_SM}}.$$
Since $\mu_{Q_SM}>8\Delta_{MS}$, we get
$$
A_S\;=\;\gamma'\mu_{Q_SM}+\gamma\Delta_{MS}\;<\;\frac{1-\gamma}{8}\mu_{Q_SM}+\frac{\gamma}{8}\mu_{Q_SM}\;=\;\frac18\mu_{Q_SM}\;\leq\;\frac12(1-\sqrt{2\gamma'})^2\mu_{Q_SM},
$$
where the last inequality follows from the fact that $\gamma'\in(0, \tfrac{1}{16})$. We conclude
\begin{equation}\label{eq:boundLQM}
    P\left(L_{Q_SM}<2\gamma'(\mu_{Q_SM}-8\Delta_{MS})\right) \;\leq\; \left(\frac12\right)^{\tfrac34|S\setminus M|}e^{-A_S} \leq e^{-A_S}
\end{equation}
Now summing the bounds in \eqref{eq:boundLQS} and \eqref{eq:boundLQM} gives the result.

\subsection{Proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:boundexpnc2}}

The proof follows the same strategy as that of Lemma~\ref{lem:boundexpnc} with differences in the distributions of the components of the difference in norms of projections. Since $M\neq T$, we have by Lemmma~\ref{lem:L0toL1convergence} (ii) $NC(M) \leq (1+e^{C(T)-C(M)})^{-1}$. By the standard formula for expectation of a nonnegative random variable in terms of its CDF, we get:
\begin{eqnarray*}
  \E\left(NC(M)\right)  &\leq& \int_0^1 P\left((1+e^{C(T)-C(M)})^{-1}\geq u\right){\rm d} u\\
  &=&\int_0^1 P\left(C(T)-C(M) \leq \ln{(\tfrac1u-1)}\right){\rm d}u\\
  &=&\int_0^1 P\Big(-\tfrac12 L_{TM} \geq \Delta_{MT}-\ln{(\tfrac1u-1)}\Big) {\rm d}u.
\end{eqnarray*}
Let $Q_T=M \cup T$ and recall that $L_{TM}=L_{Q_TM}-L_{Q_TT}$. For any $\gamma\in (\frac{1}{2},1]$
$$
-\tfrac12 L_{TM} -(\Delta_{MT}-\ln{(\tfrac1u-1)})\;=\;\left(\tfrac12 L_{Q_TT}-\ln{\Bigl(\frac{e^{\gamma\Delta_{MT}}}{\tfrac1u-1}\Bigl)}\right)-\Bigl(\tfrac12 L_{Q_TM}+(1-\gamma)\Delta_{MT}\Bigl).
$$
% \begin{equation}
%     L_{MS}-2\sum_{j=1}^b\Delta_{M,j}-2\ln{(1 / u-1)}= L_{Q_TM}-2\ln{\Bigl(\frac{e^{\gamma\sum_{j=1}^b\Delta_{M,j}}}{1 / u-1}\Bigl)}-\Bigl(L_{Q_TM}+2(1-\gamma)\sum_{j=1}^b\Delta_{M,j}\Bigl)
% \end{equation}
Observe that for any random variables $U$, $V$, and any $\epsilon,\gamma' \geq 0$, the event $
\{U-V \geq 0\}$ implies $\{U \geq \gamma' \epsilon\}\cup
\{V  < \gamma' \epsilon\}$. Let $U=\tfrac12 L_{Q_TT}-\ln{\Bigl(\frac{e^{\gamma\Delta_{MT}}}{\tfrac1u-1}\Bigl)}$ and $V=\tfrac12 L_{Q_TM}+(1-\gamma)\Delta_{MT}$. Take $\epsilon=\mu_{Q_TM}$ and $\gamma'=\tfrac18({1-\gamma})$. Recall that $A_T=\gamma\Delta_{MT}+\gamma'\mu_{Q_TM}$ and
$$
\{U\geq \gamma'\epsilon\}\;=\;\{\tfrac12 L_{Q_TT}\geq \ln{\Bigl(\frac{e^{\gamma\Delta_{MT}}}{\tfrac1u-1}}\Bigl)+\gamma'\mu_{Q_TM}\}\;=\;\{\tfrac12 L_{Q_TT}\geq \ln{\Bigl(\frac{e^{A_T}}{\tfrac1u-1}}\Bigl)\},
$$
$$
\{V<\gamma'\epsilon\}\;=\;\{\tfrac12 L_{Q_TM}<-(1-\gamma)\Delta_{MT}+\gamma'\mu_{Q_TM}\}\;=\;\{\tfrac12 L_{Q_TM}<\gamma'(\mu_{Q_TM}-8\Delta_{MT})\}.
$$
By the union bound we have:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:prooflemmas20nonspu2}
    \E\left(NC(M)\right)  \leq \int_0^1P\Bigl(\tfrac12 L_{Q_TT}\geq \ln{\Bigl(\frac{e^{A_T}}{\tfrac1u-1}\Bigl)}\Bigl){\rm d}u+ P\Bigl(\tfrac12 L_{Q_TM}<\gamma'(\mu_{Q_TM}-8\Delta_{MT})\Bigl).
\end{equation}
We first bound the first term in \eqref{eq:prooflemmas20nonspu2}. If $M \subset T$ then $Q_T=S$, $L_{Q_TT}=0$, and this term is zero. Suppose now that $T$ is a strict subset of $Q_T$. By Lemma~\ref{lemma:s7}, $L_{Q_TT}$ is distributed $\chi_{|M\setminus T|}^2(\mu_{Q_TT})$ where unlike the proof of  Lemma~\ref{lem:boundexpnc2}, $\mu_{Q_TT}$ can be positive. Let $\nu=|M\setminus T|$.

Observe that, if $M\cap S^S=\emptyset$, since $S^L\subseteq T$ and $T\cap S^I=M\cap S^I$, then $M\setminus T \subseteq V\setminus S$ and $\mu_{Q_TT}=0$. By assumption we have $A_T \geq \tfrac34|M\setminus T|=\tfrac34\nu$ and from Lemma~\ref{lemma:s20},
\begin{equation}\label{eq:boundLQS2}
\int_0^1P\Bigl(L_{Q_TT}>2\ln{\Bigl(\frac{e^{A_T}}{1 / u-1}\Bigl)}\Bigl){\rm d}u \;<\; 16
e^{-A_T}A_T^{\frac{|M\setminus T|}{2}+1}.
\end{equation}
If $M\cap S^S\neq\emptyset$ then $\mu_{Q_TT}\neq 0$. By assumption we have $\mu_{Q_TT}\geq 1$ and $\phi A_T \geq \max\{7,\tfrac12\nu\}$ and and from Lemma~\ref{lemma:s20},
\begin{equation}\label{eq:boundLQS3}
\int_0^1P\Bigl(L_{Q_TT}>2\ln{\Bigl(\frac{e^{A_T}}{1 / u-1}\Bigl)}\Bigl){\rm d}u \;<\; 2
e^{-\phi A_T}[2(\phi+1)\,A_T]^{\frac{|M\setminus T|}{4}+1}.
\end{equation}

A bound on the second term in \eqref{eq:prooflemmas20nonspu2} follows from following the same steps as in the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:boundexpnc}, replacing $S$ by $T$. 
%If $S \subset M$, then $Q_T=M$, $L_{Q_TM}=0$, $\mu_{Q_TM}=0$, and this term is zero. If $M$ is a strict subset of $Q_T$ then, by Lemma~\ref{lemma:s7}, $L_{Q_TM} \sim \chi_{|Q_T\setminus M|}^2(\mu_{Q_TM})$ %(note that $Q_T\setminus M=S\setminus M$ and $\mu_{Q_TM}=\mu_{Q_TM}$). 
% Clearly, when $\mu_{Q_TM}\leq 8\Delta_{MT}$ or $\gamma = 1$, this term is also zero so suppose  $\mu_{Q_TM}> 8\Delta_{MT}$ and $\gamma <1$.  
% We have  
% $$P\left(L_{Q_TM}<2\gamma'(\mu_{Q_TM}-8\Delta_{MT})\right)\; \leq \; P\left(L_{Q_TM}<2\gamma'\mu_{Q_TM}\right)$$ 
% and, by Lemma~\ref{lemma:s2}, as $\gamma'\in (0,\tfrac{1}{16})$
% $$P\left(L_{Q_TM}<2\gamma'\mu_{Q_TM}\right) \;\;\leq\;\; (2\gamma')^{\frac{|S\setminus M|}{4}} e^{-\frac{1}{2}(1-\sqrt{2\gamma'})^2\mu_{Q_TM}}\;\;\leq\;\;  (\tfrac12)^{\tfrac34|S\setminus M|}e^{-\frac{1}{2}(1-\sqrt{2\gamma'})^2\mu_{Q_TM}}.$$
% Since $\mu_{Q_TM}>8\Delta_{MT}$, we get
% $$
% A_T\;=\;\gamma'\mu_{Q_TM}+\gamma\Delta_{MT}\;<\;\frac{1-\gamma}{8}\mu_{Q_TM}+\frac{\gamma}{8}\mu_{Q_TM}\;=\;\frac18\mu_{Q_TM}\;\leq\;\frac12(1-\sqrt{2\gamma'})^2\mu_{Q_TM},
% $$
% where the last inequality follows from the fact that $\gamma'\in(0, \tfrac{1}{16})$. We conclude
\begin{equation}\label{eq:boundLQM2}
    P\left(L_{Q_TM}<2\gamma'(\mu_{Q_TM}-8\Delta_{MT})\right) \;\leq\; \left(\frac12\right)^{\tfrac34|T\setminus M|}e^{-A_T}\leq\;e^{-A_T}
\end{equation}
Summing the bounds in \eqref{eq:boundLQS2} and \eqref{eq:boundLQM2} gives the result for the case $M\setminus (T\cap S^I)=\emptyset$. Summing the bounds in \eqref{eq:boundLQS3} and \eqref{eq:boundLQM2} gives the result for the case $M\setminus (T\cap S^I)\neq\emptyset$.


\subsection{Proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:upperboundnoncentralgen}}
By Lemma~\ref{lemma:s7}, we have
\begin{align*}
    \mu_{Q_TT}&={\bs \beta^{\star}_{Q_T\setminus T}}^{\top}\bs X_{Q_T\setminus T}^{\top}  \big(I_n-P_T\big) \bs X_{Q_T\setminus T} \bs \beta^{\star}_{Q_T\setminus T}\\
    &={\bs \beta^{\star}_{M\setminus T}}^{\top}\bs X_{M\setminus T}^{\top}  \big(I_n-P_T\big) \bs X_{M\setminus T} \bs \beta^{\star}_{M\setminus T}\\
    &={\bs \beta^{\star}_{(S\cap M)\setminus T}}^{\top}\bs X_{(S\cap M)\setminus T}^{\top}  \big(I_n-P_T\big) \bs X_{(S\cap M)\setminus T} \bs \beta^{\star}_{(S\cap M)\setminus T}
\end{align*}
where the third equality follows from $\bs \beta^*_{M\setminus S}=0$.
We start with the upper bound. Denote for any square matrix $A$, its largest eigenvalue 
$\lambda_{\max}(A)$. We have:
$$\mu_{Q_TT}\leq n\lambda_{\max}\Big(\frac{1}{n}\bs X_{(S\cap M)\setminus T}^{\top}  \big(I_n-P_T\big) \bs X_{(S\cap M)\setminus T}\Big) \|\bs \beta^{\star}_{(S\cap M)\setminus T}\|_2^2$$
Let $B=\tfrac{1}{n}\bs X_{(S\cap M)\setminus T}^{\top}  \big(I_n-P_T\big) \bs X_{(S\cap M)\setminus T}$ and $C=\tfrac{1}{n}\bs X_{(S\cap M)\cup T}^{\top}\bs X_{(S\cap M)\cup T}$. $B$ is the Schur complement $\big(C \,  / \, \tfrac{1}{n}\bs X_T^{\top} \bs X_T\big)$. The inverse $B^{-1}$ is then a diagonal block of $C^{-1}$. By interlacing. $\lambda_{min}(B^{-1})\geq \lambda_{min}(C^{-1})$ and then $\lambda_{\max}(B)\leq \lambda_{\max}(C)$. We also have $(S\cap M)\cup T \subseteq S$, then by interlacing again $\lambda_{\max}(C)\leq \lambda_{\max}\Big(\tfrac{1}{n}\bs X_S^{\top}\bs X_S\Big)=\bar{\lambda}$. The upper bound follows from also observing that $\|\bs \beta^{\star}_{(S\cap M)\setminus T}\|_2^2\leq \sum_{j=1}^b |(S_j\cap M_j)\setminus T_j|\max_{i\in (S_j\cap M_j)\setminus T_j}{\beta_i^*}^2$.

A lower bound on $\mu_{Q_TT}$ is: 
$$\mu_{Q_TT}\geq n\lambda_{\min}\Big(\frac{1}{n}\bs X_{(S\cap M)\setminus T}^{\top}  \big(I_n-P_T\big) \bs X_{(S\cap M)\setminus T}\Big) \|\bs \beta^{\star}_{(S\cap M)\setminus T}\|_2^2$$
The final bound is obtained by similar interlacing argument as for the upper bound and the bound $\|\bs \beta^{\star}_{(S\cap M)\setminus T}\|_2^2\geq \sum_{j=1}^b |(S_j\cap M_j)\setminus T_j|\min_{i\in (S_j\cap M_j)\setminus T_j}{\beta_i^*}^2$
\subsection{Proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:convtoTprob}}

The proof of the selection consistency of $\hat{S}^b$ relied on Lemma~\ref{lem:L0toL1convergence} that show it is sufficient to study the pairwise comparison of $C(M)$ and $C(S)$ for any $M\in\mathcal{M}\setminus\{S\}$. That Lemma can be generalized to other subsets of models than $\{S\}$ and in particular we have the following result for $\mathcal{T}$.
\begin{lem}\label{lem:L0toL1convergence2}
$P(\hat{S}^{b} \not\in \mathcal{T} ) \,\,\leq \,\, (2^{|S^I|}+1) \sum_{M \in \mathcal{M}\setminus \{\mathcal{T}\}} \E\left(NC(M)\right).$
\end{lem}


\end{supplement}

\begin{supplement}
\stitle{Appendix A: Properties in the Gaussian sequence model with $s$ finite}\label{sec:appendixGSMsfinite}
\sdescription{Short description of Supplement B.}


We derive here selection properties of the block $\ell_0$ penalties in the Gaussian Sequence model dropping assumption~A3 and assuming instead:
\begin{enumerate}[start=8,label={(A\arabic*)}]
    \item $\text{for all } j, \;\; s_j \,< \, \infty$
\end{enumerate}

Changing A3 for A8 implies redeveloping results relative to the probability of false negatives and consequently betamin conditions. Observe that the Lemmas and Properties of Section~\ref{sec:GSMgen} as well as Property \ref{prop:suffnecIblockthresh} in Section~\ref{sec:properties_legression} do not depend on A3, the probability of false negatives or a betamin condition. They hold equally in the regime where $s_j$ is finite for all $j=1,\ldots,b$.

Consider the condition:
\begin{enumerate}[start=9,label={(A\arabic*)}]
    \item $\text{for all } j, \;\; \sqrt{n}(\beta_{\min,j}^{\star}-\tau_j) \to \infty$
\end{enumerate}
Under A8, A9 can be shown to be sufficient for $\hat{S}^{T,b}$ to hold the screening property. Moreover in Proposition~\ref{prop:necIIblockthresh} a) we showed A9 was a general necessary condition for the screening property without assuming A3. A9 is then necessary and sufficient for $\hat{S}^{T,b}$ to hold the property under A8.
\begin{prop}\label{prop:suffnecIIblockthreshbis}
 In the Gaussian sequence model \eqref{eq:GSM}, under assumptions A1, A2 and A9, $\hat{S}^{T,b}$ satisfies:
$$P(S\subseteq \hat{S}^{T,b} ) \to 1$$
\end{prop}
\begin{proof}
    By the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:suffnecIblockthresh}, we have: \begin{equation*}
P\left(\hat{S}^{T,b} \not\subseteq S\right)\;\leq\; \sum_{j=1}^b\frac{e^{-\tfrac{n}{2}\left((\beta^\star_{\min,j}-\tau_j)^2-\tfrac{2\ln(s_j)}{n}\right)^2}}{\sqrt{\pi\ln(s_j)}}
 \end{equation*}
 where the right-hand side goes to 0 by assumption A9.
\end{proof}
By Proposition~\ref{prop:suffnecIblockthresh}, to guarantee model recovery with high probability the block thresholds $\tau_j$ must be greater or equal to $\sqrt{2\ln(p_j-s_j)/n}$. Under A8, we then get the minimal betamin condition, for all $j=1,\ldots,b$:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:nectypeIIGSMfinite}
    \sqrt{n}\beta_{\min,j}^{\star}-\sqrt{2\ln(p_j-s_j)} \to \infty
\end{equation}
Condition \eqref{eq:nectypeIIGSMfinite} is necessary for asymptotic support recovery with $\hat{S}^{T,b}$:
\begin{lem}\label{lem:necbetaminblockthresh2}
    Suppose there exists $j\in\{1,\ldots,b\}$ such that
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:necbetaminblockthreshbis}
      \lim_{n\to \infty}\sqrt{n}\beta_{\min,j}^*-\sqrt{2\ln(p_j-s_j)} < +\infty  
    \end{equation}
then under assumptions A1, A2 and A8,\;  $P(\hat{S}^{T,b} = S)\not \to 1$
\end{lem}

\begin{proof}

  We have $\sqrt{n}\beta_{\min,j}^*-\sqrt{2\ln(p_j-s_j)} \,=\, \sqrt{n}\beta_{\min,j}^*-\sqrt{n}\tau_j+\sqrt{2\ln(p_j-s_j)}\Big(\tfrac{\sqrt{n}\tau_j}{\sqrt{2\ln(p_j-s_j)}}-1\Big)$ and \eqref{eq:necbetaminblockthreshbis} implies that there exists $c\in\R^+$ such that:
  \begin{equation}\label{eq:proofnecbetaminbis}
      \lim_{n\to \infty}\sqrt{n}\beta_{\min,j}^*-\sqrt{n}\tau_j+\sqrt{2\ln(p_j-s_j)}\Big(\tfrac{\sqrt{n}\tau_j}{\sqrt{2\ln(p_j-s_j)}}-1\Big)\leq c
  \end{equation}

Consider the case $\lim_{n\to +\infty}\tfrac{\sqrt{n}\tau_j}{\sqrt{2\ln(p_j-s_j)}}-1<0$. Then by Proposition~\ref{prop:suffnecIblockthresh},
$P(\hat{S}^{T,b} = S)\not \to 1$. 
Now consider the case $\lim_{n\to +\infty}\tfrac{\sqrt{n}\tau_j}{\sqrt{2\ln(p_j-s_j)}}-1 \geq0$. Then by \eqref{eq:proofnecbetaminbis},  $\lim_{n\to \infty}\sqrt{n}\beta_{\min,j}^*-\sqrt{n}\tau_j \leq c$ and by Proposition~\ref{prop:necIIblockthresh} a), $P(\hat{S}^{T,b} = S)\not \to 1$.
\end{proof}

Theorem~\ref{theo:fullrecovGS} does not assume A3 but requires the earlier betamin assumption~A5. Now under A8, A9 implies A5. As a result, when $s_j$ is finite for all $j=1,\ldots,b$, Theorem~\ref{theo:fullrecovGS} holds equally assuming A8 and A9 instead of A5.

It follows that the gains in terms of convergence rate with  informed block thresholding under A8 are the same as under A3. To discuss gains in terms of betamin conditions, we assume without loss of generality that $\beta_{\min}^{\star}$ is located in block $b$. By \eqref{eq:nectypeIIGSMfinite}, the minimal betamin conditions with block informed thresholding $\hat{S}^{T,b}$ and standard thresholding $\hat{S}^1$ are respectively:
\begin{align}
    \sqrt{n}\beta_{\min,b}^{\star}-\sqrt{2\ln(p_b-s_b)} \to \infty \quad\text{and}\quad
    \sqrt{n}\beta_{\min}^{\star}-\sqrt{2\ln(p-s)} \to \infty
\end{align}
The relevant quantities to compare the minimal requirement on the growth rate of betamin are thus $\sqrt{\tfrac{\ln(p_b-s_b)}{n}}$ and $\sqrt{\tfrac{\ln(p-s)}{n}}$. Since $p_b-s_b \leq p - s$, the minimal betamin condition with $\hat{S}^{T,b}$ is always milder than with $\hat{S}^{T,1}$ under A8 again. Moreover, observe that under Scenarios 1 to 3 in Section~\ref{sec:examples}, the relevant ratio to compare minimal betamin condition was also $\Theta\Big(\sqrt{\tfrac{\ln(p_b-s_b)}{\ln(p-s)}}\Big)$. As a result, those scenarios also illustrate the magnitude of the gains in terms of minimal betamin conditions under A8.

\end{supplement}


\begin{supplement}
\stitle{Appendix B: Non linear block $\ell_0$ penalty in high-dimensional linear regression}\label{sec:appendixnonlinear}
\sdescription{Short description of Supplement C.}

In this appendix we derive selection properties of the block $\ell_0$ penalties in the linear regression setting without assuming linearity. 

Let $\eta_j$ for $j=1,\ldots,b$ be any non-negative and increasing penalty functions on the natural numbers. The selector based on those $\ell_0$ block penalties is:
\begin{equation}
  \widehat S^b\;\in\;\arg\max_{M\in \mathcal{M}} \left\{\max_{\beta\in \mathcal{L}_M} \ell(\bs y;\bs \beta)-\sum_{j=1}^b\eta_j (|M_j|)\right\},\qquad M=M_1\cup \ldots\cup M_b.	
\end{equation}
where $\ell$ is the log-likelihood function.

Rewrite the difference in penalty between any model $M$ and the true model $S$:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:DeltaMbis}
\Delta_{MS} \; := \; \sum_{j=1}^b \eta_j(|M_j|)-\eta_j(|S_j|)
\end{equation}
%The proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:boundexpnc} does not rely on the linearity of block penalties and the Lemma holds identically with the redefined $\Delta_{M}$. 
We define the normalized difference in block penalty between any model $M$ and the true support $S$ as:
 $$\tilde{\Delta}_{M, j}\; := \; \begin{cases}
     \frac{\eta_j(|M_j|)-\eta_j(|S_j|)}{|M_j|\,-\,|S_j|} \;\;\; \text{if}\;\;\; |M_j|\,\neq\,|S_j| \\
     0 \;\;\;\text{if}\;\;\; |M_j|\,=\,|S_j|
 \end{cases}  $$
The quantity $\tilde{\Delta}_{M, j}$ plays a similar role as $\kappa_j$, representing the average penalty incurred for adding a variable from block $B_j$ to the model $M$. However, unlike in the case of linear penalties,  $\tilde{\Delta}_{M, j}$ is not constant in $|M|$. For any $M\in\mathcal{M}$, $\tilde{\Delta}_{M, j}$ is exactly $\kappa_j$ if the penalty is linear as defined in Section \ref{sec:intro_blockpen}.

As in the linear case, consistency is achieved by setting conditions on the $\tilde{\Delta}_{M, j}$ and $\beta^*_{\min, j}$:
\begin{enumerate}[start=9,label={(A\arabic*)}]
    \item for each $j$, there exist $b_{j}\to \infty$ (as $n\to \infty$) such that for all $M \in \mathcal{M}$ such that $|M_j| \neq |S_j|$ and $|M_j \setminus S_j| >0$, for $n$ sufficiently large, and  all $j=1,\ldots,b$:
\begin{equation}\label{cond:suffIregnonlinpen}
    \tilde{\Delta}_{M, j} =\;\ln\bigg(\frac{p_j-s_j}{|M_j \setminus S_j| }\bigg) + b_{j}
\end{equation}

\item  for each $j$, there exist $c_{j}\to +\infty$ such that for $n$ sufficiently large, for all $j=1,\ldots,b$
    \begin{equation}\label{cond:suffIIregnonlinpen}
        \sqrt{\frac{(1-\gamma) n\rho(\bs X)}{8}}{{\beta_{\min}^{\star}}_j} \,- \,\sqrt{\ln(p_j-s_j) + b_{j}} \;=\; \sqrt{\ln(s_j)} + c_{j} .
    \end{equation}
where $\alpha_j:=\frac{\ln(p_j-s_j)}{\ln(p_j-s_j) + b_{j}}$ and $\gamma:=\frac12(1+\max_j \alpha_j)\in (\tfrac12,1)$ for $n$ large enough.
\end{enumerate}
\begin{thm}\label{theo:suffcondlinearmodelnonlinpen}
Suppose that A1, A2, A7 and A10 hold, then 
    % \begin{align}
    %  &\qquad \tilde{\Delta}_{M, j} > \ln(p_j-s_j)  \label{cond:suffIregnonlinpen}\\
    %    &\qquad \sqrt{\frac{2(1-\gamma) n\rho(\bs X)}{8}}{{\beta_{\min}^{\star}}_j}  \geq \sqrt{\tilde{\Delta}_{M, j}}+{\sqrt{\ln(s_j)}}  .\label{cond:suffIIregnonlinpen}
    % \end{align}
Then $$\sum_{M \in \mathcal{M}\setminus \{S\}}\E\left(NC(M)\right)\to 0 \qquad \mbox{and} \qquad P(\widehat{S}^b = S) \to 1$$
\end{thm}

\begin{proof}
The proof is essentially the same as proof Theorem~\ref{theo:suffcondlinearmodel} replacing $\kappa_j$ by $\tilde{\Delta}_{M, j}$ for every $j=1,\ldots,b$. We rewrite here the main intermediate results of the proof Theorem~\ref{theo:suffcondlinearmodel} and redevelop only the steps altered by the non-linearity of the penalty. We refer the reader to the proof of Theorem~\ref{theo:suffcondlinearmodel} for more details.

Observe that $\Delta_{MS}$, defined in \eqref{eq:DeltaMbis}, can be rewritten as $\Delta_{MS}=\sum_{j=1}^b(|M_j\setminus S_j|-|S_j\setminus M_j|)\tilde{\Delta}_{M}$. By  Lemma~\ref{lem:boundrho}, for every $n\in \N$ we have 
\begin{equation}\label{eq:nonoverfittingcond}
   A_S\;=\; \gamma \Delta_{MS}+\frac{1-\gamma}{8}\mu_{Q_SM}\;\geq\; \gamma\sum_{j=1}^b|M_j \setminus S_j|\tilde{\Delta}_{M} + \sum_{j=1}^b |S_j\setminus M_j |\left(\tfrac{1-\gamma}{8} n \rho(\bs X) {\beta^{\star}_{\min,j}}^{2} - \gamma\tilde{\Delta}_{M, j}\right) 
\end{equation} 
As for Theorem~\ref{theo:suffcondlinearmodel}, by assumption A10 we have $\gamma\sum_{j}|M_j\setminus S_j|\tilde{\Delta}_{M, j}\geq \tfrac{3}{4}|M\setminus S|$ as long as $\min_j \ln(p_j-s_j)\geq \tfrac32$, which always holds in our asymptotic regime for $n$ sufficiently large. Observe that the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:boundexpnc} does not rely on the linearity of block penalties and the Lemma holds identically with the redefined $\Delta_{MS}$.  By Lemma~\ref{lem:boundexpnc} we have:
$$
\E(NC(M))\;\leq\;16 e^{-A_S}A_S^{|M\setminus S|/2+1}+e^{-A_S},
$$
where $A_S=\gamma\Delta_{MS}+\tfrac{1-\gamma}{8}\mu_{Q_SM}$. Similarly to Theorem~\ref{theo:suffcondlinearmodel}, defining:
$$A_S^*\;:=\;\gamma\sum_{j=1}^b|M_j \setminus S_j|\tilde{\Delta}_{M, j} + \sum_{j=1}^b |S_j\setminus M_j |\left(\tfrac{1-\gamma}{8} n \rho(\bs X) {\beta^{\star}_{\min,j}}^{2} - \gamma\tilde{\Delta}_{M, j}\right) $$
we get 
$$
\E(NC(M))\;\leq\;16 e^{-A_S^*}{A_S^*}^{|M\setminus S|/2+1}+e^{-A_S^*}.
$$
Note that 
$$A_S^*\;=\;\gamma\sum_{j \in \{1,\ldots, b\} \text{s.t.} |M_j \setminus S_j| \neq 0}^b|M_j \setminus S_j|\tilde{\Delta}_{M, j} + \sum_{j=1}^b |S_j\setminus M_j |\left(\tfrac{1-\gamma}{8} n \rho(\bs X) {\beta^{\star}_{\min,j}}^{2} - \gamma\tilde{\Delta}_{M, j}\right) $$
Furthermore, condition \eqref{cond:suffIIregnonlinpen} implies there exist $c^{\prime}_j \to +\infty$ such that:
$$\frac{(1-\gamma) n\rho(\bs X)}{8}{{\beta_{\min}^{\star}}_j}^2 \,- \,\tilde{\Delta}_{M, j} \;=\; \ln(s_j) + c^{\prime}_{j}.$$
We also have that for $x$ sufficiently large and any $\psi \in (0,1)$
\begin{equation}%\label{eq:boundexp}
16e^{-x}x^{\tfrac12|M\setminus S|+1}+e^{-x}\leq e^{-\psi x}.    
\end{equation}
To choose $\psi$ we proceed as in the proof of Theorem~\ref{theo:suffcondlinearmodel}. Let $\delta \in (0,1)$ and denote:
$$\bar{m}_j=\begin{cases}
    \max\bigg\{\tfrac{2\ln\big(\tfrac{p_j-s_j}{|M_j\setminus S_j|}\big)}{b_{j}},\tfrac{2\ln(s_j)}{c^{\prime}_{j}}\bigg\},\;\;\; \text{if}\;\;|M_j \setminus S_j| \neq 0 \\
    \frac{2\ln(s_j)}{c^{\prime}_{j}},\;\;\; \text{if}\;\;|M_j \setminus S_j| \neq 0
\end{cases}$$
Take $\psi=\max_{j=1, \ldots,b}\frac{a + \delta + \bar{m}}{1+\bar{m}}$ for some $a \in(0, 1- \delta)$ then $\psi \in (0,1)$ and for every $j=1,\ldots,b$:
$$\delta \leq \psi-(1-\psi)\bar{m}_j.$$ 
By definition of $\gamma$ and $\alpha_j$, we have for every $j=1,\ldots,b$ such that $|M_j \setminus S_j|\neq0$:
$$
\gamma\tilde{\Delta}_{M, j}\;\geq\;\tfrac12 (1+\alpha_j)\tilde{\Delta}_{M, j}\geq \tfrac12 \bigg(1+\tfrac{\ln\big(\tfrac{p_j-s_j}{|M_j\setminus S_j|}\big)}{\tilde{\Delta}_{M, j}}\bigg)\tilde{\Delta}_{M, j} \;=\; \ln\big(\tfrac{p_j-s_j}{|M_j\setminus S_j|}\big)+ \tfrac12b_{j}.
$$
From our choice of $\psi,\delta$, we further get:
$$\psi\gamma\tilde{\Delta}_{M, j} \;\geq\; \psi\bigg(\ln\big(\tfrac{p_j-s_j}{|M_j\setminus S_j|}\big)+ \frac12b_{j}\bigg) \;\geq\; \ln\big(\tfrac{p_j-s_j}{|M_j\setminus S_j|}\big)+ \delta\frac12b_{j}.$$
Observe that, for any $M$, $\tilde{\Delta}_{M, j} \leq \ln(p_j-s_j)+b_j$. Then, by \eqref{cond:suffIIregnonlinpen} and our choice of $\psi,\delta$:
$$\psi\left(\tfrac{1-\gamma}{8} n \rho(\bs X) {\beta^{\star}_{\min,j}}^{2} - \gamma\tilde{\Delta}_{M, j}\right) \;\geq\; \psi\left(\ln(s_j)+c_j'\right) \;\geq\; \ln(s_j)+\delta \frac{c^{\prime}_j}{2}.
$$
Using the expression for $A_S^*$, the bound in \eqref{eq:boundexp}, and the above inequalities, we get
$$
\E(NC(M))\;\leq\;\exp\{- \sum_{j \in \{1,\ldots, b\} \text{s.t.} |M_j \setminus S_j| > 0} |M_j\setminus S_j|\bigg(\ln\bigg(\frac{p_j-s_j}{|M_j\setminus S_j|}\bigg)+\delta \tfrac{b_j}{2}\bigg)-\sum_{j=1}^b |S_j\setminus M_j|(\ln(s_j)+\delta \tfrac{c^{\prime}_j}{2})\}.
$$
Following the same subsequent steps of the proof of Theorem~\ref{theo:suffcondlinearmodel} shows selection consistency.
\end{proof}

The EBIC penalty is equivalent to considering that all the variables belong to a single block, that is $b=1$, and setting $\eta_1(|M|)=\psi\ln{p\choose |M|} + \tfrac{1}{2}\ln(n)$. The EBIC penalty can be shown to satisfy assumption~A10 whenever $s=o(\ln(n))$ and the following corollary holds:
\begin{cor}
    Suppose that $b=1$, $\eta_1(|M|)=\psi \ln {p\choose |M|} + \tfrac{1}{2}\ln(n)$ with $\psi\geq1$, $s=o(\sqrt{n})$, $\max|M|_{M\in\mathcal{M}} \leq \tfrac{p}{2}$ and that there exists $c\to +\infty$ such that for $n$ sufficiently large
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:betaminebic}
        \sqrt{\frac{\ln(n)-2(s-2)}{2 \ln(p)+\ln(n)-2(s-2)}\frac{n\rho(\bs X)}{16}}{{\beta_{\min}^{\star}}} \,- \,\sqrt{\psi\ln(p) + \frac{1}{2}\ln(n)} \;=\; \sqrt{\ln(s)} + c .
    \end{equation}
    Then A10 and A11 hold and under A1 and A2: $$\sum_{M \in \mathcal{M}\setminus \{S\}}\E\left(NC(M)\right)\to 0 \qquad \mbox{and} \qquad P(\widehat{S}^b = S) \to 1$$    
\end{cor}

\begin{proof}
    We start by showing $\eta_1$ satisfies assumption~A10. Let $M\in\mathcal{M}$ and denote $|M\setminus S|=u$ and $|S\setminus M|=w$. We have $|M|=u+s-w$ and $|M|-|S|=u-w$. As in A10 consider the case $u-w>0$ and $u>0$, then:
    \begin{equation*}
        \tilde{\Delta}_{M,1}=\frac{\psi}{u-w}\ln\bigg[\frac{{p \choose |M|}}{{p \choose |S|}}\bigg]+ \frac{1}{2}\ln n
    \end{equation*}
    A well-known property of binomial coefficients is that for any positive integers $n$, $h$, $k$ we have:
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:idbinom}
        \binom{n}{h}\binom{n-h}{k}=\binom{n}{h+k}\binom{h+k}{h}
    \end{equation}
    Consider first the case in which $u-w>0$, take $n=p$, $k=u-w$ and $h=s$ in \eqref{eq:idbinom}, we have:
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:identitybinom}
        \frac{{p \choose |M|}}{{p \choose s}}=
        %\frac{{n \choose h+k}}{{n \choose h}}= \frac{{n -h\choose k}}{{h+k \choose h}} =
        \frac{{p -s \choose u-w}}{{|M| \choose s}}
    \end{equation}
    % \begin{equation}\label{eq:identitybinom}
    %     \frac{{p \choose |M|}}{{p \choose s}}=\frac{{p \choose p-s+w-u}}{{p \choose s}} =
    %     \frac{{n \choose h+k}}{{n \choose n-h}}=\frac{{n \choose h+k}}{{n \choose h}}= \frac{{n -h\choose k}}{{h+k \choose h}} =
    %     \frac{{p -s \choose u-w}}{{|M| \choose s}}
    % \end{equation}
A standard bounds on binomial coefficient for $1\leq k\leq n$ are:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:boundbinom}
  \left(\frac{n}{k}\right)^k \leq  {n \choose k} \leq \frac{n^n}{k^k(n-k)^{n-k}}
\end{equation}
Combining \eqref{eq:identitybinom} and \eqref{eq:boundbinom}, we get:
$$ \frac{\psi}{u-w}\ln\bigg[\frac{{p \choose |M|}}{{p \choose s}}\bigg] \geq \psi\ln\bigg(\frac{p-s}{u-w}\bigg)-\psi\bigg[\frac{s}{u-w}\ln\bigg(\frac{u-w}{s}+1\bigg) +\ln\bigg(1+\frac{s}{u-w}\bigg) \bigg]$$
We have:
$$\psi\ln\bigg(\frac{p-s}{u-w}\bigg) \geq \psi\ln\bigg(\frac{p-s}{u}\bigg) \geq \ln\bigg(\frac{p-s}{u}\bigg)$$
Observe also that $f:x \mapsto \frac{\ln(x+1)}{x}+\ln(1+{x}^{-1})$ for $x>0$ is decreasing. We have $\frac{u-w}{s} \geq s^{-1}$ and then: 
\begin{equation}\label{eq:lowerboundb1ebic2}
        \tilde{\Delta}_{M,1} \geq \ln\big(\frac{p-s}{u}\big)-s\ln(s^{-1}+1)+ \ln \big(\tfrac{\sqrt{n}}{1+s}\big)
\end{equation}
 We have that $\lim_{n\to+\infty}s\ln(s^{-1}+1)< +\infty$ and since $s=o(\sqrt{n})$, $\ln(\tfrac{\sqrt{n}}{s+1}) \to +\infty$, then $\eta_1$ satisfies assumption~A10 in the case $u-w>0$.\\
 Consider now the case where $u-w<0$. We have:
     \begin{equation*}
        \tilde{\Delta}_{M,1}=\frac{\psi}{w-u}\ln\bigg[\frac{{p \choose s}}{{p \choose |M|}}\bigg]+ \frac{1}{2}\ln n
    \end{equation*}
 Taking $n=p$, $k=w-u$ and $h=|M|$ in \eqref{eq:idbinom}, we obtain:
     \begin{equation}\label{eq:identitybinom}
        \frac{{p \choose s}}{{p \choose |M|}}=
        %\frac{{n \choose h+k}}{{n \choose h}}= \frac{{n -h\choose k}}{{h+k \choose h}} =
        \frac{{p - |M| \choose w-u}}{{s \choose |M|}}
    \end{equation}
By bounds in \eqref{eq:boundbinom}, we get:
$$ \frac{\psi}{w-u}\ln\bigg[\frac{{p \choose s}}{{p \choose |M|}}\bigg] \geq \psi\ln\bigg(\frac{p-|M|}{w-u}\bigg)+\psi\bigg[\frac{s}{w-u}\ln\bigg(1-\frac{w-u}{s}\bigg) -\ln\bigg(\frac{s}{w-u}-1\bigg) \bigg]$$
Since $|M|<s\leq p/2$ and $\psi\geq 1$ we have:
$$\psi\ln\bigg(\frac{p-|M|}{w-u}\bigg) \geq \psi\ln\bigg(\frac{p-s}{w-u}\bigg) \geq \psi\ln\bigg(\frac{p-s}{w}\bigg)\geq \ln\bigg(\frac{p-s}{u}\bigg)$$
Observe that $g:x \mapsto \frac{\ln(1-x)}{x}-\ln({x}^{-1}-1)$ for $x\in(0,1)$ is increasing. We have $\frac{w-u}{s} \geq s^{-1}$ and then: 
\begin{equation}
        \tilde{\Delta}_{M,1} \geq \ln\big(\frac{p-s}{u}\big)-s\ln(1-s^{-1})+ \ln \big(\tfrac{\sqrt{n}}{s-1}\big)
\end{equation}
Note that the righ-handside above is well defined. Since $u>0$ and $u-w<0$, we have $w\geq2$ and $|M|<s$. The later is not possible unless $s-1>0$. We have that $\lim_{n\to+\infty}s\ln(1-s^{-1})< +\infty$ and since $s=o(\sqrt{n})$, $\ln(\tfrac{\sqrt{n}}{s-1}) \to +\infty$, then $\eta_1$ satisfies assumption~A10 in the case $u-w<0$ too.

 We now show that if \eqref{eq:betaminebic} holds then A11 holds. Since A10 holds there exists $b_1$ such that for all $M\in\mathcal{M}$ such that $|M|\neq s$ and $|M\setminus S|>0$, $\tilde{\Delta}_{M, 1} =\;\ln\bigg(\frac{p-s}{|M \setminus S| }\bigg) + b_{1}$. By inequalities \eqref{eq:lowerboundb1ebic1} and \eqref{eq:lowerboundb1ebic2}, for any of those $M$, $b_1 \geq \tfrac{1}{2}\ln(n)-(s+2)$. By assumption~A11 we have: $1-\gamma=1-\frac{1}{2}\frac{\ln(p-s)}{})$ XXX -MISSING XXX


\end{proof}
\end{supplement}
